1	---
2	component: FocusScope
3	category: utility
4	tier: stateful

11	
12	# FocusScope
13	
14	FocusScope constrains keyboard Tab focus within a container, enabling focus trapping for
15	modal dialogs, drawers, and other overlay components that must prevent focus from escaping.
16	
17	## 1. State Machine
18	
19	### 1.1 States
20	
21	| State                      | Description                                                    |
22	| -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
23	| `Inactive`                 | Focus scope is idle; Tab behavior is unmodified.               |
24	| `Active { trapped: bool }` | Focus scope is active. `trapped=true` means Tab cannot escape. |
25	
26	### 1.2 Events
27	
28	| Event          | Payload               | Description                                                      |
29	| -------------- | --------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
30	| `Activate`     | `trapped: bool`       | Activate the focus scope, optionally trapping focus.             |
31	| `Deactivate`   | `restore_focus: bool` | Deactivate the scope and optionally restore previous focus.      |
32	| `TrapFocus`    | —                     | Enable focus trapping on an active scope.                        |
33	| `ReleaseTrap`  | —                     | Disable focus trapping on an active scope.                       |
34	| `RestoreFocus` | —                     | Restore focus to the element that was focused before activation. |
35	| `FocusFirst`   | —                     | Move focus to the first tabbable element in the container.       |
36	| `FocusLast`    | —                     | Move focus to the last tabbable element in the container.        |
37	
38	### 1.3 Context
39	
40	```rust
41	/// The states for the `FocusScope` component.
42	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
43	pub enum State {
44	    /// Focus scope is idle; Tab behavior is unmodified.
45	    Inactive,
46	    /// Focus scope is active. `trapped=true` means Tab cannot escape.
47	    Active {
48	        /// When true, Tab cannot escape the container.
49	        trapped: bool,
50	    },
51	}
52	
53	/// The events for the `FocusScope` component.
54	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
55	pub enum Event {
56	    /// Activate the focus scope, optionally trapping focus.
57	    /// The adapter captures the currently focused element ID before sending this event
58	    /// (via `platform.active_element_id()`) so the machine can store it for later restoration.
59	    Activate {
60	        /// When true, Tab cannot escape the container.
61	        trapped: bool,
62	        /// ID of the element that had focus before activation (for restore-on-deactivate).
63	        /// Captured by the adapter via `platform.active_element_id()`.
64	        saved_focus_id: Option<String>,
65	    },
66	    /// Deactivate the scope and optionally restore previous focus.
67	    Deactivate {
68	        /// When true, restore focus to the previously focused element.
69	        restore_focus: bool,
70	    },
71	    /// Enable focus trapping on an active scope.
72	    TrapFocus,
73	    /// Disable focus trapping on an active scope.
74	    ReleaseTrap,
75	    /// Restore focus to the element that was focused before activation.
76	    /// Only processed during deactivation (via `then_send` from Deactivate).
77	    /// Ignored if the scope is still Active.
78	    RestoreFocus,
79	    /// Move focus to the first tabbable element in the container.
80	    FocusFirst,
81	    /// Move focus to the last tabbable element in the container.
82	    FocusLast,
83	}
84	
85	// FocusScope props (`trapped`, `contain`, `auto_focus`, `restore_focus`) are read
86	// at activation time and are immutable during the active lifecycle. To change
87	// trapping behavior, deactivate and reactivate the scope.
88	
89	/// The context for the `FocusScope` component.
90	///
91	/// **Note:** `active` and `trapped` are NOT stored in context. They are derived
92	/// from `State` in the connect API:
93	/// - `is_active()` → `matches!(state, State::Active { .. })`
94	/// - `is_trapped()` → `matches!(state, State::Active { trapped: true })`
95	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
96	pub struct Context {
97	    /// The element that had focus before the scope was activated.
98	    /// Restored on `Deactivate` if `restore_focus=true`.
99	    pub saved_focus: Option<String>,
100	    /// The DOM ID of the container element that scopes focus.
101	    pub container_id: Option<String>,
102	}
103	```
104	
105	### 1.4 Props
106	
107	```rust
108	/// Props for the `FocusScope` component.
109	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, HasId)]
110	pub struct Props {
111	    /// Component instance ID.
112	    pub id: String,
113	    /// Prevent Tab from moving focus outside the container.
114	    pub trapped: bool,
115	    /// Alias for trapped (clearer naming in some contexts).
116	    pub contain: bool,
117	    /// On activation, automatically move focus to the first tabbable element
118	    /// (or the element with autofocus attribute if present).
119	    pub auto_focus: bool,
120	    /// On deactivation, restore focus to the previously focused element.
121	    pub restore_focus: bool,
122	}
123	
124	impl Default for Props {
125	    fn default() -> Self {
126	        Self {
127	            id: String::new(),
128	            trapped: false,
129	            contain: false,
130	            auto_focus: true,
131	            restore_focus: true,
132	        }
133	    }
134	}
135	```
136	
137	### 1.5 Transitions
138	
139	```text
140	Inactive + Activate { trapped }
141	  → Active { trapped: trapped || contain }
142	  action: save currently focused element → ctx.saved_focus
143	  effect: "focus-trap-listener" (attaches keydown handler to intercept Tab)
144	  then_send: FocusFirst (if auto_focus=true)
145	
146	Active + Deactivate { restore_focus }
147	  → Inactive
148	  action: clear ctx.saved_focus reference
149	  cleanup effect: remove keydown handler
150	  then_send: RestoreFocus (if restore_focus=true)
151	
152	Active { trapped: false } + TrapFocus
153	  → Active { trapped: true }
154	
155	Active { trapped: true } + ReleaseTrap
156	  → Active { trapped: false }
157	
158	Inactive + RestoreFocus
159	  → Inactive (stay)
160	  action: restore focus from ctx.saved_focus via restore_focus_safely()
161	
162	Active + RestoreFocus
163	  → None (ignored — RestoreFocus is only meaningful after Deactivate)
164	
165	Active + FocusFirst
166	  → Active (stay)
167	  effect: focus first tabbable element in container
168	
169	Active + FocusLast
170	  → Active (stay)
171	  effect: focus last tabbable element in container
172	
173	When `contain` is true and no tabbable elements exist within the scope, FocusScope MUST:
174	  (1) keep focus on the container element (which has `tabindex="-1"`),
175	  (2) suppress Tab/Shift+Tab key events entirely (`preventDefault()`),
176	  (3) re-scan for tabbable elements on each Tab press to detect dynamically added
177	      content (e.g., lazy-loaded dialog body).
178	  (handled entirely in effect, no state change needed)
179	```
180	
181	### 1.6 Full Machine Implementation
182	
183	```rust
184	use ars_core::{TransitionPlan, PendingEffect, ConnectApi, AttrMap};
185	
186	/// The machine for the `FocusScope` component.
187	pub struct Machine;
188	
189	/// This component has no translatable strings.
190	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
191	pub struct Messages;
192	impl ComponentMessages for Messages {}
193	
194	impl ars_core::Machine for Machine {
195	    type State = State;
196	    type Event = Event;
197	    type Context = Context;
198	    type Props = Props;
199	    type Messages = Messages;
200	    type Api<'a> = Api<'a>;
201	
202	    fn init(props: &Props, _env: &Env, _messages: &Messages) -> (State, Context) {
203	        (
204	            State::Inactive,
205	            Context {
206	                saved_focus: None,
207	                container_id: None,
208	            },
209	        )
210	    }
211	
212	    fn transition(
213	        state: &Self::State,
214	        event: &Self::Event,
215	        ctx: &Self::Context,
216	        props: &Self::Props,
217	    ) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Self>> {
218	        match (state, event) {
219	            // ── Activation ──────────────────────────────────────────────
220	            (State::Inactive, Event::Activate { trapped, saved_focus_id }) => {
221	                let trap = *trapped || props.contain;
222	                let auto_focus = props.auto_focus;
223	                let saved = saved_focus_id.clone();
224	                let mut plan = TransitionPlan::to(State::Active { trapped: trap })
225	                    .apply(move |ctx| {
226	                        ctx.saved_focus = saved;
227	                    })
228	                    .with_named_effect("focus-trap-listener", |ctx, _props, send| {
229	                        let platform = use_platform_effects();
230	                        let container_id = ctx.ids.part("container");
231	                        platform.attach_focus_trap(&container_id, Box::new(move || {
232	                            send.call_if_alive(Event::Deactivate { restore_focus: true });
233	                        }))
234	                    });
235	                if auto_focus {
236	                    plan = plan.then(Event::FocusFirst);
237	                }
238	                Some(plan)
239	            }
240	
241	            // ── Deactivation ────────────────────────────────────────────
242	            (State::Active { .. }, Event::Deactivate { restore_focus }) => {
243	                let restore = *restore_focus;
244	                let mut plan = TransitionPlan::to(State::Inactive);
245	                if restore {
246	                    // RestoreFocus will read saved_focus then clear it.
247	                    plan = plan.then(Event::RestoreFocus);
248	                } else {
249	                    plan = plan.apply(|ctx| {
250	                        ctx.saved_focus = None; // Clear to prevent stale DOM references
251	                    });
252	                }
253	                Some(plan)
254	            }
255	
256	            // ── Trap / Release ──────────────────────────────────────────
257	            (State::Active { trapped: false }, Event::TrapFocus) => {
258	                Some(TransitionPlan::to(State::Active { trapped: true }))
259	            }
260	            (State::Active { trapped: true }, Event::ReleaseTrap) => {
261	                Some(TransitionPlan::to(State::Active { trapped: false }))
262	            }
263	
264	            // ── RestoreFocus ────────────────────────────────────────────
265	            // Only processed when Inactive (after Deactivate via then_send).
266	            (State::Inactive, Event::RestoreFocus) => {
267	                Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(|ctx| {
268	                    if let Some(ref target) = ctx.saved_focus {
269	                        restore_focus_safely(target.clone(), &[]);
270	                    }
271	                    ctx.saved_focus = None;
272	                }))
273	            }
274	            // If RestoreFocus arrives while Active, ignore it.
275	            (State::Active { .. }, Event::RestoreFocus) => None,
276	
277	            // ── Focus Navigation ────────────────────────────────────────
278	            (State::Active { .. }, Event::FocusFirst) => {
279	                Some(TransitionPlan::new()
280	                    .with_named_effect("focus_first", |ctx, _props, _send| {
281	                        let platform = use_platform_effects();
282	                        if let Some(ref id) = ctx.container_id {
283	                            platform.focus_first_tabbable(id);
284	                        }
285	                    }))
286	            }
287	            (State::Active { .. }, Event::FocusLast) => {
288	                Some(TransitionPlan::new()
289	                    .with_named_effect("focus_last", |ctx, _props, _send| {
290	                        let platform = use_platform_effects();
291	                        if let Some(ref id) = ctx.container_id {
292	                            platform.focus_last_tabbable(id);
293	                        }
294	                    }))
295	            }
296	
297	            _ => None,
298	        }
299	    }
300	
301	    // **Modality coordination:** programmatic focus restoration MUST preserve the
302	    // shared `ModalityContext` state rather than forcing pointer modality.
303	    // This ensures `data-ars-focus-visible` remains correct — programmatic focus
304	    // should only show a focus ring when the prior interaction was not pointer-driven.
305	
306	    fn connect<'a>(
307	        state: &'a Self::State,
308	        ctx: &'a Self::Context,
309	        props: &'a Self::Props,
310	        send: &'a dyn Fn(Self::Event),
311	    ) -> Self::Api<'a> {
312	        Api { state, ctx, props, send }
313	    }
314	}
315	```
316	
317	#### 1.6.1 Focus Restoration Safety
318	
319	````rust
320	// Guard against restoring focus to a removed or unfocusable element.
321	//
322	// Checks performed before restoring focus:
323	// 1. Element is in the DOM (document.contains)
324	// 2. Element is visible (not visibility:hidden, not display:none)
325	// 3. Element is not inside a closed <details> element
326	// 4. Element is not already the active element (document.activeElement)
327	// 5. Element can receive focus (is tabbable or has tabindex)
328	// 6. Element has layout (offsetParent != null)
329	//
330	// If the target fails any check, try each fallback in order, then the
331	// nearest focusable ancestor, then the document body.
332	//
333	// The `fallbacks` parameter supports nested dialog scenarios where the
334	// original trigger may have been removed. Callers pass a prioritized list
335	// (e.g., [parent_dialog_last_focused, parent_dialog_container,
336	// parent_dialog_first_focusable]) so the function can gracefully degrade.
337	fn restore_focus_safely(target_id: &str, fallback_ids: &[&str]) {
338	    let platform = use_platform_effects();
339	    if platform.can_restore_focus(target_id) {
340	        platform.focus_element_by_id(target_id);
341	        return;
342	    }
343	    // Try fallback elements in order
344	    for id in fallback_ids {
345	        if platform.can_restore_focus(id) {
346	            platform.focus_element_by_id(id);
347	            return;
348	        }
349	    }
350	    // Walk up to nearest focusable ancestor of the original target.
351	    if let Some(ancestor_id) = platform.nearest_focusable_ancestor_id(target_id) {
352	        platform.focus_element_by_id(&ancestor_id);
353	    } else {
354	        // Last resort — focus document body
355	        platform.focus_body();
356	    }
357	}
358	
359	// ── Orientation Change Focus Audit ──────────────────────────────────────
360	//
361	// When the viewport orientation changes (e.g., portrait → landscape on mobile),
362	// CSS media queries may hide or show elements. If FocusScope has trapped focus
363	// on an element that is now hidden by `display: none`, the element stays in
364	// the DOM but is invisible and has `offsetParent == null`.
365	//
366	// The FocusScope Tab handler MUST check `offsetParent !== null` before allowing
367	// focus on any element. Additionally, the adapter MUST register a
368	// `matchMedia('(orientation: portrait)')` change listener that triggers a
369	// focus audit when orientation changes:
370	//
371	//   1. Check if the currently focused element has `offsetParent == null`.
372	//   2. If hidden, move focus to the first visible tabbable element in the scope.
373	//   3. Update the saved_focus reference if the restore target is now hidden.
374	//
375	// ```rust
376	// fn audit_focus_on_orientation_change(scope: &FocusScopeContext) {
377	//     let platform = use_platform_effects();
378	//     if let Some(active_id) = platform.active_element_id() {
379	//         if !platform.has_layout(&active_id) {
380	//             // Focused element is hidden — move to first visible tabbable
381	//             if let Some(ref container_id) = scope.container_id {
382	//                 platform.focus_first_tabbable(container_id);
383	//             }
384	//         }
385	//     }
386	//     // Also validate the saved_focus restore target
387	//     if let Some(ref saved) = scope.saved_focus {
388	//         if !platform.has_layout(saved) {
389	//             // Restore target is now hidden; clear it so fallback chain is used
390	//             scope.saved_focus = None;
391	//         }
392	//     }
393	// }
394	// ```
395	````
396	
397	### 1.7 Connect / API
398	
399	```rust
400	#[derive(ComponentPart)]
401	#[scope = "focus-scope"]
402	pub enum Part {
403	    Container,
404	}
405	
406	/// The API for the `FocusScope` component.
407	pub struct Api<'a> {
408	    /// The current state of the focus scope.
409	    state: &'a State,
410	    /// The context of the focus scope.
411	    ctx: &'a Context,
412	    /// The props of the focus scope.
413	    props: &'a Props,
414	    /// The send function for the focus scope.
415	    send: &'a dyn Fn(Event),
416	}
417	
418	impl<'a> Api<'a> {
419	    /// Whether the focus scope is active.
420	    pub fn is_active(&self) -> bool {
421	        matches!(self.state, State::Active { .. })
422	    }
423	
424	    /// Whether the focus scope is trapped.
425	    pub fn is_trapped(&self) -> bool {
426	        matches!(self.state, State::Active { trapped: true })
427	    }
428	
429	    /// Props for the container element that scopes focus.
430	    pub fn container_attrs(&self) -> AttrMap {
431	        let mut attrs = AttrMap::new();
432	        let [(scope_attr, scope_val), (part_attr, part_val)] = Part::Container.data_attrs();
433	        attrs.set(scope_attr, scope_val);
434	        attrs.set(part_attr, part_val);
435	        if self.is_active() {
436	            attrs.set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-active"), true);
437	
438	            // tabindex="-1" allows the container itself to be focused programmatically,
439	            // which is needed as a focus target when no tabbable children exist yet.
440	            // Only set when active — an inactive scope's container should not appear
441	            // as a programmatic focus target to screen readers.
442	            attrs.set(HtmlAttr::TabIndex, "-1");
443	        }
444	        if self.is_trapped() {
445	            attrs.set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-trapped"), true);
446	        }
447	        // Event handlers (keydown for Tab trapping) are typed methods on the Api struct.
448	        attrs
449	    }
450	
451	    /// Imperatively activate the focus scope.
452	    /// `saved_focus_id` is the ID of the currently focused element, captured by
453	    /// the adapter via `platform.active_element_id()` before calling this.
454	    pub fn activate(&self, trapped: bool, saved_focus_id: Option<String>) {
455	        (self.send)(Event::Activate { trapped, saved_focus_id });
456	    }
457	
458	    /// Imperatively deactivate the focus scope.
459	    pub fn deactivate(&self, restore_focus: bool) {
460	        (self.send)(Event::Deactivate { restore_focus });
461	    }
462	
463	    /// Move focus to the first tabbable element within the container.
464	    /// Used by framework adapters when auto_focus=true.
465	    pub fn focus_first(&self) {
466	        let platform = use_platform_effects();
467	        if let Some(ref id) = self.ctx.container_id {
468	            platform.focus_first_tabbable(id);
469	        }
470	    }
471	
472	    /// Move focus to the last tabbable element within the container.
473	    pub fn focus_last(&self) {
474	        let platform = use_platform_effects();
475	        if let Some(ref id) = self.ctx.container_id {
476	            platform.focus_last_tabbable(id);
477	        }
478	    }
479	
480	    /// Return IDs of all currently tabbable elements in the container.
481	    pub fn get_tabbable_elements(&self) -> Vec<String> {
482	        let platform = use_platform_effects();
483	        self.ctx.container_id
484	            .as_ref()
485	            .map(|id| platform.tabbable_element_ids(id))
486	            .unwrap_or_default()
487	    }
488	
489	    /// Move focus to the next focusable element within the scope.
490	    /// Returns `true` if focus was moved, `false` if no valid target exists.
491	    /// Used by Toolbar, ActionGroup, and TreeView for programmatic sequential
492	    /// focus movement beyond roving tabindex.
493	    pub fn focus_next(&self, _opts: FocusNavigationOptions) -> bool {
494	        // IMPL: traverse tabbable elements forward within scope
495	        false
496	    }
497	
498	    /// Move focus to the previous focusable element within the scope.
499	    /// Returns `true` if focus was moved, `false` if no valid target exists.
500	    pub fn focus_previous(&self, _opts: FocusNavigationOptions) -> bool {
501	        // IMPL: traverse tabbable elements backward within scope
502	        false
503	    }
504	}
505	
506	impl ConnectApi for Api<'_> {
507	    type Part = Part;
508	
509	    fn part_attrs(&self, part: Part) -> AttrMap {
510	        match part {
511	            Part::Container => self.container_attrs(),
512	        }
513	    }
514	}
515	
516	/// Options for focus navigation.
517	#[derive(Clone)]
518	pub struct FocusNavigationOptions {
519	    /// Wrap around at boundaries.
520	    pub wrap: bool,
521	    /// Element ID to start from (default: currently focused element).
522	    pub from: Option<String>,
523	    /// Only consider tabbable elements (tabindex >= 0).
524	    pub tabbable: bool,
525	    /// Custom filter predicate that receives an element ID.
526	    /// Uses `Rc` so the options struct can be cloned.
527	    pub accept: Option<Rc<dyn Fn(&str) -> bool>>,
528	}
529	```
530	
531	#### Focus Manager Context
532	
533	The adapter SHOULD provide the FocusScope's navigation methods (`focus_next`, `focus_previous`, `focus_first`, `focus_last`) via framework context, allowing child components to programmatically manage focus without prop drilling. In Leptos: `provide_context(FocusManager { ... })`. In Dioxus: `use_context_provider(|| FocusManager { ... })`. This mirrors React Aria's `useFocusManager()` hook pattern.
534	
535	## 2. Anatomy
536	
537	```text
538	FocusScope
539	└── Container    <div> (or any element)    data-ars-scope="focus-scope"
540	                                           data-ars-part="container"
541	                                           data-ars-active (when active)
542	                                           data-ars-trapped (when trapped)
543	```
544	
545	| Part      | Element          | Key Attributes                                                                                     |
546	| --------- | ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
547	| Container | `<div>` (or any) | `data-ars-scope="focus-scope"`, `data-ars-part="container"`, `data-ars-active`, `data-ars-trapped` |
548	
549	## 3. Accessibility
550	
551	### 3.1 ARIA Roles, States, and Properties
552	
553	- No ARIA role is set on the container — FocusScope is a behavioral utility, not a semantic landmark.
554	- `tabindex="-1"` is set on the container when active, allowing programmatic focus when no tabbable children exist.
555	- `data-ars-active` and `data-ars-trapped` are data attributes for styling hooks; they are not ARIA attributes.
556	
557	### 3.2 Focus Management
558	
559	- A focus trap is required for modal dialogs per ARIA 1.2 (APG Modal Dialog pattern).
560	- The scope must include a way to close it reachable by keyboard (typically Escape key handled
561	  by the parent component, not by `FocusScope` itself).
562	- `auto_focus` moves focus into the dialog on open, which is required for screen reader users
563	  to know the dialog has appeared.
564	- `restore_focus` returns focus to the trigger on close, maintaining orientation in the page.
565	
566	## 4. Internationalization
567	
568	Label text is consumer-provided. `data-ars-*` attribute values are stable API tokens, not localized. RTL: no special handling needed — focus order follows DOM order regardless of text direction.
569	
570	## 5. Tabbable Element Detection
571	
572	The tabbable element query matches (in DOM order):
573	
574	```css
575	a[href]:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
576	button:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
577	input:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
578	select:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
579	textarea:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
580	[contenteditable]:not([tabindex="-1"]),
581	[tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])
582	```
583	
584	Elements with `visibility:hidden`, `display:none`, or inside a `<details>` (closed) are excluded.
585	
586	## 6. Usage by ars-ui Components
587	
588	| Component          | Props                                                  |
589	| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------ |
590	| `Dialog`           | `trapped=true, restore_focus=true, auto_focus=true`    |
591	| `AlertDialog`      | `trapped=true, restore_focus=true, auto_focus=true`    |
592	| `Drawer`           | `trapped=true, restore_focus=true, auto_focus=true`    |
593	| `Popover`          | `trapped=false, restore_focus=true, auto_focus=true`   |
594	| `Combobox` listbox | `trapped=false, restore_focus=false, auto_focus=false` |
595	
596	### Composition with Dismissable
597	
598	When a component uses both FocusScope (trapping focus) and Dismissable (providing DismissButton), the DismissButton elements MUST be rendered as children of the FocusScope container element. Placing DismissButton as a sibling of the FocusScope container makes it unreachable by Tab when focus is trapped.
599	
600	**Correct:**
601	
602	```html
603	<div data-ars-scope="dialog">
604	    <!-- FocusScope container -->
605	    <DismissButton />
606	    <!-- Inside trap — reachable -->
607	    <div data-ars-part="content">...</div>
608	    <DismissButton />
609	    <!-- Inside trap — reachable -->
610	</div>
611	```
612	
613	**Incorrect:**
614	
615	```html
616	<DismissButton />
617	<!-- Outside trap — unreachable! -->
618	<div data-ars-scope="dialog">
619	    <div data-ars-part="content">...</div>
620	</div>
621	```
622	
623	## 7. Focus Restoration Fallbacks
624	
625	1. If the original focus target has been removed from the DOM, focus moves to `document.body` and a console warning is logged.
626	2. If the focus target is in a different document (iframe), focus stays in the current document — cross-document focus restoration is not attempted.
627	3. If the document has no focusable elements, `document.body.focus()` is called as final fallback.
628	4. Focus restoration is always synchronous (in the same microtask as trap release).
629	
630	## 8. Nested `FocusScope` Restoration
631	
632	When multiple `FocusScope`s are nested (e.g., a dialog opens a confirmation popover), focus
633	restoration must follow a strict LIFO (last-in, first-out) order. Each scope pushes its
634	restore target onto a shared stack when activated and pops it on deactivation.
635	
636	### 8.1 Focus Restoration Stack
637	
638	The stack is shared via `thread_local!` with `RefCell` for web targets (single-threaded). Each document gets its own stack instance. In multi-document environments (iframes), the `ArsProvider` context determines which document's stack to use.
639	
640	```rust
641	/// Global (per-document) stack tracking nested FocusScope restore targets.
642	/// Managed by the adapter's FocusScope effect layer, not by individual
643	/// state machines.
644	#[derive(Default, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
645	pub struct FocusRestorationStack {
646	    /// The entries in the focus restoration stack.
647	    entries: Vec<FocusRestoreEntry>,
648	}
649	
650	/// An entry in the focus restoration stack.
651	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
652	pub struct FocusRestoreEntry {
653	    /// The FocusScope instance that pushed this entry.
654	    pub scope_id: String,
655	    /// The element that held focus before this scope activated.
656	    pub saved_focus: Option<ElementId>,
657	    /// The element that triggered this scope's activation (e.g., the button
658	    /// that opened a dialog). Used as the primary restore target.
659	    pub trigger_element: Option<ElementId>,
660	}
661	
662	impl FocusRestorationStack {
663	    /// Creates a new focus restoration stack.
664	    pub fn new() -> Self {
665	        Self { entries: Vec::new() }
666	    }
667	
668	    /// Called when a FocusScope activates. Records the current focus target.
669	    pub fn push(&mut self, scope_id: String, trigger: Option<ElementId>) {
670	        let saved = document().active_element_id();
671	        self.entries.push(FocusRestoreEntry {
672	            scope_id,
673	            saved_focus: saved,
674	            trigger_element: trigger,
675	        });
676	    }
677	
678	    /// Called when a FocusScope deactivates. Returns the element to restore
679	    /// focus to, applying the fallback chain.
680	    pub fn pop(&mut self, scope_id: &str) -> Option<ElementId> {
681	        // Find and remove the entry for this scope.
682	        let idx = self.entries.iter().rposition(|e| e.scope_id == scope_id)?;
683	        let entry = self.entries.remove(idx);
684	
685	        // Out-of-order deactivation check: if this scope is NOT the top of
686	        // the stack, its saved target may reside inside a now-inactive outer
687	        // scope. In that case, skip restoration entirely — the outer scope
688	        // will handle it when it deactivates.
689	        if idx < self.entries.len() {
690	            // Inner scope deactivating after an outer scope was already removed.
691	            // The saved target is likely invalid. Return None to let the caller
692	            // fall through to the fallback chain.
693	            return None;
694	        }
695	
696	        // Fallback chain:
697	        // 1. Saved target, if it still exists in the DOM and is focusable.
698	        if let Some(ref el_id) = entry.saved_focus {
699	            if is_valid_restore_target(el_id) {
700	                return Some(el_id.clone());
701	            }
702	        }
703	        // 2. Trigger element, if valid.
704	        if let Some(ref trigger_id) = entry.trigger_element {
705	            if is_valid_restore_target(trigger_id) {
706	                return Some(trigger_id.clone());
707	            }
708	        }
709	        // 3. First tabbable element in the parent scope (caller handles).
710	        // 4. document.body (caller handles as final fallback).
711	        None
712	    }
713	}
714	
715	/// Validates that an element still exists in the DOM and is focusable.
716	fn is_valid_restore_target(el_id: &ElementId) -> bool {
717	    let Some(el) = document().get_element_by_id(el_id) else {
718	        return false;
719	    };
720	    // Element must be connected to the DOM and not hidden/disabled.
721	    el.is_connected() && is_focusable(&el)
722	}
723	```
724	
725	**SSR safety:** The `FocusRestorationStack` is a client-only construct. Adapters MUST gate its initialization:
726	
727	- **Leptos:** Wrap in `#[cfg(not(feature = "ssr"))]` or guard with `leptos::is_server()` check
728	- **Dioxus:** Initialize inside `use_effect` (which only runs on the client)
729	
730	If the stack is accidentally created on the server (e.g., via a global `thread_local!`), it could retain stale state across SSR requests in multi-tenant server environments.
731	
732	**Nested restoration priority:** When both an inner and outer scope deactivate (e.g., a
733	confirmation popover closes followed by its parent dialog), the inner scope restores first,
734	then the outer scope restores — each popping from the stack in LIFO order.
735	
736	**`saved_focus` validation:** Before restoring focus to a saved element, the adapter MUST
737	verify that the element (a) still exists in the DOM via `is_connected()` and (b) is
738	focusable (not `disabled`, not `display:none`, has valid `tabindex`). If validation fails,
739	the fallback chain proceeds.
740	
741	**Out-of-order deactivation:** If an inner scope deactivates after its outer scope has
742	already been removed (e.g., both close simultaneously but cleanup runs in arbitrary order),
743	the inner scope's saved target may point to an element inside the now-destroyed outer scope.
744	In this case, the stack skips the inner scope's restoration and defers to the outer scope's
745	fallback chain.
746	
747	**Fallback chain** (in priority order):
748	
749	1. The saved focus target, if still valid in the DOM and focusable
750	2. The scope's trigger element (the element that caused the scope to activate)
751	3. The first tabbable element in the parent scope
752	4. `document.body` as the final fallback
753	
754	## 9. Platform Notes
755	
756	> **Dioxus focus operations:** Focus operations (`focus_first`, `focus_last`,
757	> `restore_focus_safely`) use `PlatformEffects` trait methods (see `01-architecture.md`
758	> section 2.2.7). For Dioxus Desktop/Mobile, the adapter provides a platform implementation
759	> that routes these through native focus APIs for cross-platform compatibility.
760	>
761	> **Cleanup timing:** Leptos uses `on_cleanup` and Dioxus uses `use_drop` for teardown.
762	> In HMR/hot-reload scenarios, timing may differ — ensure the `FocusRestorationStack`
763	> is cleared on both cleanup and re-mount.
764	
765	## 10. Library Parity
766	
767	> Compared against: React Aria (`FocusScope`).
768	
769	### 10.1 Props
770	
771	| Feature       | ars-ui                | React Aria     | Notes                                    |
772	| ------------- | --------------------- | -------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
773	| Auto-focus    | `auto_focus`          | `autoFocus`    | Both libraries                           |
774	| Contain/trap  | `trapped` / `contain` | `contain`      | Both libraries; ars-ui offers both names |
775	| Restore focus | `restore_focus`       | `restoreFocus` | Both libraries                           |
776	
777	**Gaps:** None.
778	
779	### 10.2 Anatomy
780	
781	| Part      | ars-ui      | React Aria    | Notes                                  |
782	| --------- | ----------- | ------------- | -------------------------------------- |
783	| Container | `Container` | (wrapper div) | Both libraries use a container element |
784	
785	**Gaps:** None.
786	
787	### 10.3 Features
788	
789	| Feature                  | ars-ui                              | React Aria              |
790	| ------------------------ | ----------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
791	| Focus trapping           | Yes                                 | Yes                     |
792	| Focus restoration        | Yes                                 | Yes                     |
793	| Auto-focus first element | Yes                                 | Yes                     |
794	| FocusManager context     | Yes (`focus_next`/`focus_previous`) | Yes (`useFocusManager`) |
795	| Nested scope stack       | Yes (`FocusRestorationStack`)       | Yes (internal)          |
796	| Focus navigation options | Yes (`FocusNavigationOptions`)      | Yes (`wrap`, etc.)      |
797	
798	**Gaps:** None.
799	
800	### 10.4 Summary
801	
802	- **Overall:** Full parity.
803	- **Divergences:** ars-ui exposes both `trapped` and `contain` prop aliases. ars-ui explicitly defines `FocusRestorationStack` for nested scopes; React Aria handles this internally.
804	- **Recommended additions:** None.
805	
```

## Investigation Report: FocusScope PlatformEffects Infrastructure

Based on a medium-depth codebase review, here's the status of the infrastructure referenced by the focus-scope spec:

### 1. **PlatformEffects Trait Status**

**Location:** `/crates/ars-core/src/platform.rs` (lines 21-165)

**Already Defined Methods:**
All 10 focus-related methods listed in your spec **are already defined** on the trait:

| Method | Signature | Line |
|--------|-----------|------|
| `focus_element_by_id` | `fn focus_element_by_id(&self, id: &str)` | 36 |
| `focus_first_tabbable` | `fn focus_first_tabbable(&self, container_id: &str)` | 39 |
| `focus_last_tabbable` | `fn focus_last_tabbable(&self, container_id: &str)` | 42 |
| `tabbable_element_ids` | `fn tabbable_element_ids(&self, container_id: &str) -> Vec<String>` | 48 |
| `focus_body` | `fn focus_body(&self)` | 51 |
| `active_element_id` | `fn active_element_id(&self) -> Option<String>` | 114 |
| `attach_focus_trap` | `fn attach_focus_trap(&self, container_id: &str, on_escape: Box<dyn Fn()>) -> Box<dyn FnOnce()>` | 118 |
| `can_restore_focus` | `fn can_restore_focus(&self, id: &str) -> bool` | 122 |
| `nearest_focusable_ancestor_id` | `fn nearest_focusable_ancestor_id(&self, id: &str) -> Option<String>` | 126 |

**Missing Method:**
One method referenced in the spec **does not exist**:
- `has_layout(id) -> bool` — mentioned in spec §1.6.1 (line 379) and orientation-change audit (line 706), but NOT on `PlatformEffects`. This would need to be added.

**NullPlatformEffects:** Yes, it's the test/SSR default at lines 210–338 with full no-op implementations.

---

### 2. **use_platform_effects() Function**

**Location:** `ars-leptos/src/provider.rs` and `ars-dioxus/src/provider.rs`

Example from ars-leptos (lines 2–13):
```rust
pub fn use_platform_effects() -> Arc<dyn PlatformEffects> {
    current_ars_context().map_or_else(
        || -> Arc<dyn PlatformEffects> {
            warn_missing_provider("use_platform_effects");
            Arc::new(ars_core::MissingProviderEffects)
        },
        |ctx| -> Arc<dyn PlatformEffects> { Arc::clone(&ctx.platform) },
    )
}
```

**Availability:** Always available in effect closures inside adapters. It requires an `ArsProvider` context to be installed (wrapper component at app root). If missing, it falls back to `MissingProviderEffects` which emits debug warnings (no-op) in debug builds.

No crate-level cfgs gate it — it's available in all adapters.

---

### 3. **CleanupFn and no_cleanup()**

**Location:** `crates/ars-core/src/lib.rs`

```rust
pub type CleanupFn = Box<dyn FnOnce()>;

#[inline]
#[must_use]
pub fn no_cleanup() -> CleanupFn {
    Box::new(|| {})
}
```

This replaces the old `Box::new(|| {})` pattern directly — it's a simple type alias and factory function.

---

### 4. **PendingEffect and with_named_effect**

**Location:** `crates/ars-core/src/lib.rs`

**PendingEffect struct** (lines ~150–170):
- Holds a `name: M::Effect`, optional `target_state`, optional `metadata`
- The `setup` field is a closure: `FnOnce(&M::Context, &M::Props, WeakSend<M::Event>) -> CleanupFn`

**with_named_effect signature** (lines ~180–188):
```rust
pub fn with_named_effect(
    self,
    name: M::Effect,
    setup: impl FnOnce(&M::Context, &M::Props, WeakSend<M::Event>) -> CleanupFn + 'static,
) -> Self
```

The closure receives context, props, and a weak send handle to avoid retain cycles. It must return a `CleanupFn`.

**Service::take_initial_effects:** Defined in the `Service<M>` struct (lines ~230–250). It drains effects created during `Machine::initial_effects()` on first mount, before any events are processed.

---

### 5. **ComponentIds / HasId / ids.part("container")**

**Location:** `crates/ars-core/src/component_ids.rs`

This is **real API**:
- `ComponentIds::from_id(base_id)` creates an ID helper from an adapter-provided hydration-safe base (e.g., `use_id()` in Leptos).
- `ids.part("container")` returns `"{base}-{part}"` — e.g., `"dialog-3-container"` (line 52–54)
- `ids.item("item", &key)` returns `"{base}-{item}-{key}"` for keyed items

Used throughout the codebase: e.g., `ars-a11y/src/label.rs` line 47 shows `self.ids.part("label")`.

---

### 6. **document() abstraction vs PlatformEffects**

**Status:** Mixed pattern — **spec sketch vs. real code differ**

- In the spec, `document().active_element_id()` appears in `FocusRestorationStack::push` (line 670).
- **In reality:** There is no global `document()` abstraction in `ars-core`. The spec's `FocusRestorationStack` is conceptual/pseudo-code, not yet implemented in the core.
- **Actual pattern:** ars-dom provides focus utilities at `/crates/ars-dom/src/focus.rs` (e.g., `active_element_id_for_platform()`, `can_restore_focus_for_platform()`) that ARE routed through `PlatformEffects` methods by web adapters.

The spec notation `document().active_element_id()` is intended as high-level pseudocode; the real implementation calls `platform.active_element_id()`.

---

### 7. **ars-dom focus utilities**

**Location:** `crates/ars-dom/src/focus.rs` (lines 1–343)

ars-dom exports **platform-facing wrapper functions** that adapters use:
- `active_element_id_for_platform()` → wraps `active_element_id_impl()` (line 297)
- `can_restore_focus_for_platform(id)` → wraps `can_restore_focus_impl(id)` (line 318)
- `tabbable_element_ids_for_platform(container_id)` → wraps `tabbable_element_ids_impl()` (line 311)
- `nearest_focusable_ancestor_id_for_platform(id)` → wraps `nearest_focusable_ancestor_id_impl()` (line 325)

These are **NOT called by the agnostic core directly** — they're for adapters to implement `PlatformEffects`.

The core machine calls `use_platform_effects()` and invokes trait methods. The adapter (e.g., `ars-dom`) implements `PlatformEffects` using these ars-dom utilities under the hood.

---

## Summary: What's Missing

Before implementing FocusScope's core machine, add:

1. **`has_layout(id: &str) -> bool`** to `PlatformEffects` trait and both `NullPlatformEffects` and `MissingProviderEffects` (spec §1.6.1, line 379; orientation change audit, line 706).

2. **No touching of `NullPlatformEffects`** beyond the above — it's the SSR/test default already in place.

All other infrastructure is present: trait methods exist, `use_platform_effects()` is wired in adapters, `CleanupFn`/`no_cleanup()` pattern is established, `with_named_effect` closure signature is correct, `ComponentIds.part()` is real, and ars-dom provides focus utilities that adapters route through `PlatformEffects`.

## API Investigation Report: `TransitionPlan` and Related Types in `ars-core`

Based on my investigation of `/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7b271fe9/crates/ars-core/src/lib.rs` and related modules, here are the exact APIs:

### 1. **TransitionPlan<M> Constructors** (lines 336–394)
- `TransitionPlan::to(state)` — **exists**, const fn returning plan with target state
- `TransitionPlan::new()` — **exists**, const fn returning empty plan (no state change)
- `TransitionPlan::context_only(closure)` — **exists**, creates a plan with only context mutation

### 2. **TransitionPlan Builder Methods** (lines 365–428)
- `.apply(f: impl FnOnce(&mut M::Context) + 'static)` — chains context mutations; multiple calls chain sequentially
- `.with_effect(effect: PendingEffect<M>)` — adds a single effect; can be called multiple times
- `.with_named_effect(name: M::Effect, setup: impl FnOnce(...) + 'static)` — convenience builder for inline effects; **can be chained** (multiple `.with_named_effect(...)` calls stack)
- `.then(event: M::Event)` — enqueues a follow-up event; **supports multiple** (stored in `then_send: Vec<M::Event>`)
- `.cancel_effect(name: M::Effect)` — cancels a named effect; **can chain multiple cancellations**

### 3. **Effect Closure Signature** (line 412–418)
```rust
pub fn with_named_effect(
    self,
    name: M::Effect,
    setup: impl FnOnce(&M::Context, &M::Props, WeakSend<M::Event>) -> CleanupFn + 'static,
) -> Self
```
- `ctx`: `&<M as Machine>::Context` (shared immutable reference)
- `props`: `&<M as Machine>::Props` (shared immutable reference)
- `send`: `WeakSend<M::Event>` (weak reference; caller bridges to `StrongSend` internally)
- **Return type**: `CleanupFn` (which is `Box<dyn FnOnce()>`)

### 4. **PendingEffect<M> Structure** (lines 210–229)
```rust
pub struct PendingEffect<M: Machine> {
    pub name: M::Effect,
    pub target_state: Option<M::State>,
    pub metadata: Option<EffectMetadata>,
    pub(crate) setup: EffectSetupFn<M>,  // internal setup closure
}
```
Created via:
- `PendingEffect::new(name, setup_closure)` — takes weak-send closure
- `PendingEffect::named(name)` — marker-only with no-op setup
- `PendingEffect::named_with_metadata(name, metadata)` — marker + structured metadata

### 5. **Effect Cleanup Type** (lines 109–117)
```rust
pub type CleanupFn = Box<dyn FnOnce()>;

pub fn no_cleanup() -> CleanupFn {
    Box::new(|| {})
}
```
- `no_cleanup()` is a public function returning an empty `CleanupFn`

### 6. **Machine::initial_effects Signature** (lines 828–834)
```rust
fn initial_effects(
    _state: &Self::State,
    _context: &Self::Context,
    _props: &Self::Props,
) -> Vec<PendingEffect<Self>> {
    Vec::new()
}
```
- Default implementation returns empty vector
- Called once per mount via `Service::take_initial_effects()`

### 7. **Service::take_initial_effects()** (lines 1132–1138)
```rust
pub fn take_initial_effects(&mut self) -> Vec<PendingEffect<M>> {
    if let Some(inputs) = self.initial_inputs.take() {
        M::initial_effects(&inputs.state, &inputs.context, &self.props)
    } else {
        Vec::new()
    }
}
```
- Called exactly once per component lifecycle
- Deterministic — snapshotted inputs captured at `Service::new()` (not current state)

### 8. **Machine::transition Signature** (lines 786–791)
```rust
fn transition(
    state: &Self::State,
    event: &Self::Event,
    context: &Self::Context,
    props: &Self::Props,
) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Self>>;
```
- Returns `Option<TransitionPlan<Self>>`
- `None` = event ignored; `Some(plan)` = transition applied

### 9. **ConnectApi Trait** (lines 621–627)
```rust
pub trait ConnectApi {
    type Part: ComponentPart;
    fn part_attrs(&self, part: Self::Part) -> AttrMap;
}
```
- Single method: `part_attrs(&self, part: Self::Part) -> AttrMap`

### 10. **AttrMap API** (lines 2123–2280)
Methods include:
- `AttrMap::new()` — creates empty map
- `.set(attr: HtmlAttr, value: impl Into<AttrValue>)` — sets/replaces attribute
- `.set_bool(attr: HtmlAttr, bool)` — shorthand for boolean attributes
- `.set_style(prop: CssProperty, value: impl Into<String>)` — sets CSS property
- `.merge(&mut self, other: AttrMap)` — merges another map (last-write-wins, space-separated dedup)
- `.get()`, `.contains()`, `.iter_attrs()`, `.iter_styles()` — accessors

### 11. **HtmlAttr Enum** (lines 230–656)
Comprehensive enum with **no dedicated variants** for `data-ars-scope`, `data-ars-part`, `data-ars-active`, `data-ars-trapped`. Instead:
- `HtmlAttr::Data(&'static str)` — parameterized variant for arbitrary `data-*` attributes
- Per `ComponentPart::data_attrs()` (line 609–614), the macro generates:
  ```rust
  [
      (HtmlAttr::Data("ars-scope"), Self::scope()),
      (HtmlAttr::Data("ars-part"), self.name()),
  ]
  ```
- **Custom data attributes required**: You'll use `HtmlAttr::Data("ars-active")`, `HtmlAttr::Data("ars-trapped")` in your `part_attrs()` implementation

### Key Takeaway
The API is **fully composed** — no new enum variants needed for FocusScope. Use `HtmlAttr::Data(...)` for any custom data attributes, and the architecture supports arbitrarily chained effects, context mutations, and follow-up events through builder combinators.

## Investigation Report: FocusScope Agnostic Core Patterns

Based on thorough examination of the ars-ui workspace, here are the key patterns you must follow for implementing FocusScope at `/crates/ars-components/src/utility/focus_scope/mod.rs`:

### 1. **Existing Utility Components** (directory structure only)
The workspace has **12 utility components** at `/crates/ars-components/src/utility/`:
- **stateless:** `form`, `landmark`, `visually_hidden`, `separator`, `highlight`, `group`, `heading`, `download_trigger`
- **stateful:** `dismissable` (state machine), `live_region` (state machine with effects)
- All use **directory-backed `mod.rs`** format (no separate child files in those examined).

### 2. **Module File Structure Pattern**
- **Organization:** All examined components (dismissable, live_region) use **single `mod.rs`** file at module root — no split into separate state/event/context files.
- **Tests:** Tests **live inline in `mod.rs`** inside `#[cfg(test)]` mod at the bottom, not in `crates/ars-components/tests/`.
- **Snapshots:** Insta snapshots stored in `<component>/snapshots/` subdirectory with auto-generated filenames like:
  - `ars_components__utility__dismissable__tests__dismissable_root_default.snap`
  - Format: YAML header with source/expression, followed by debug output of the tested object.

### 3. **`Part` Enum Pattern** (Dismissable as reference)
```rust
#[derive(ComponentPart)]
#[scope = "dismissable"]
pub enum Part {
    Root,
    DismissButton,
}
```
- `#[derive(ComponentPart)]` macro (from `ars_core`) auto-generates:
  - `scope()` → returns `"dismissable"` (from `#[scope = "..."]`)
  - `name()` → returns kebab-case variant names: `"root"`, `"dismiss-button"`
  - `data_attrs()` → returns `[(scope_attr, scope_val), (part_attr, part_val)]` tuple for unpacking
  - `all()` → returns `Vec<Self>` of all variants
- Call via: `Part::Root.data_attrs()` returns `[("data-ars-scope", "dismissable"), ("data-ars-part", "root")]` (actual attr keys are `HtmlAttr::Data("ars-scope")` etc., but snapshots show rendered form).
- **Data attributes output:** Sets `data-ars-scope="<scope>"` and `data-ars-part="<name>"` automatically.

### 4. **State Machine Implementation** (Live Region as reference)
**File:** `/crates/ars-components/src/utility/live_region/mod.rs` lines 200–291

Structure:
```rust
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Machine;

impl ars_core::Machine for Machine {
    type State = State;
    type Event = Event;
    type Context = Context;
    type Props = Props;
    type Messages = Messages;
    type Effect = Effect;
    type Api<'a> = Api<'a>;

    fn init(props: &Self::Props, _env: &Env, _messages: &Self::Messages) 
        -> (Self::State, Self::Context) {
        // Return initial state + context
    }

    fn transition(
        state: &Self::State,
        event: &Self::Event,
        ctx: &Self::Context,
        props: &Self::Props,
    ) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Self>> {
        // Pattern-match (state, event) and return plans
    }
```

**`TransitionPlan` usage** (live_region line 245–252):
```rust
TransitionPlan::to(State::Announcing)
    .apply(move |ctx: &mut Context| {
        ctx.messages.clear();
        ctx.pending_message = Some(message);
        ctx.current_priority = priority;
    })
    .with_effect(announce_delay_effect())
```

**Key methods:**
- `TransitionPlan::to(state)` — sets target state
- `.apply(|ctx: &mut Context| {...})` — context mutation closure (chainable)
- `.with_effect(effect)` — adds a `PendingEffect<Machine>` (chainable)
- `.context_only(|ctx: &mut Context| {...})` — when only context changes, no state change (line 259)
- `TransitionPlan::context_only(...)` — shorthand for no-state-change plans
- `.cancel_effect(Effect::Variant)` — remove effect by tag (line 469)

**Effect naming** (line 476–478):
```rust
fn announce_delay_effect() -> PendingEffect<Machine> {
    PendingEffect::named(Effect::AnnounceDelay)
}
// Then used: .with_effect(announce_delay_effect())
```
Effects use an enum (e.g., `Effect::AnnounceDelay`, `Effect::RenderAnnouncement`) and are tagged via `PendingEffect::named()` for cancellation and description.

### 5. **Test File Conventions**
- **Location:** Tests **inline in `mod.rs`** after code, wrapped in `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { ... }` (dismissable line 504+)
- **Snapshot tests:** Use `insta::assert_snapshot!(name, value)` (dismissable line 1033–1076):
  ```rust
  #[test]
  fn dismissable_root_default_snapshot() {
      assert_snapshot!(
          "dismissable_root_default",
          snapshot_attrs(&api(Props::new()).root_attrs())
      );
  }
  ```
- **Snapshot files:** Auto-generated in `snapshots/` subdir; reviewed/committed to repo
- **CI gate:** `cargo xtask ci snapshot-count` enforces **per-component budget** (xtask/src/ci/mod.rs lines 619–644):
  - Min **3 snapshots per variant** (spec policy)
  - **Budget formula:** `min(per_part_per_variant × variants × parts, max_per_component)` where per-part-per-variant=3, max=40
  - For simple utilities (Presence, Dismissable, FocusScope): typically hit the variant×parts×3 formula, not the 40 ceiling
- **Proptest:** State-machine invariant tests in `crates/ars-components/tests/proptest_state_machines/utility.rs` (centralized, not per-component)

### 6. **`AttrMap` / `HtmlAttr` / `ConnectApi` Pattern**
**From dismissable lines 484–502 (dismiss_button_attrs):**
```rust
pub fn dismiss_button_attrs(label: impl Into<AttrValue>) -> AttrMap {
    let mut attrs = AttrMap::new();
    let [(scope_attr, scope_val), (part_attr, part_val)] = 
        Part::DismissButton.data_attrs();

    attrs
        .set(scope_attr, scope_val)
        .set(part_attr, part_val)
        .set(HtmlAttr::Role, "button")
        .set(HtmlAttr::Type, "button")
        .set(HtmlAttr::TabIndex, "0")
        .set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Label), label)
        .set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-visually-hidden"), true);
    attrs
}
```

**Key patterns:**
- `AttrMap::new()` creates empty map
- `.set(key, value)` chains attribute writes
- `.set_bool(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-visually-hidden"), true)` sets presence-only data attrs
- `HtmlAttr::TabIndex` — enum variant for `tabindex` attribute (defined in `ars_core`)
- `HtmlAttr::Data("key")` wraps `data-*` attributes
- `HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Label)` wraps ARIA attributes

**ConnectApi implementation** (live_region lines 422–429):
```rust
impl ConnectApi for Api<'_> {
    type Part = Part;

    fn part_attrs(&self, part: Part) -> AttrMap {
        match part {
            Part::Root => self.root_attrs(),
        }
    }
}
```
Dispatches to part-specific `*_attrs()` helper methods.

**State attribute** (live_region line 370):
```rust
.set(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-state"), self.data_state())
// where data_state() returns match on State:
//   State::Idle => "idle"
//   State::Announcing => "announcing"
```
Outputs `data-ars-state="<state>"` for CSS/test selectors.

### 7. **Snapshot Test Count Enforcement**
- **CI step:** `cargo xtask ci snapshot-count` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` line ~139)
- **Configuration:** `xtask/src/ci/mod.rs` lines 620–643:
  - `snapshots_dir: crates` (scans all crate snapshot dirs)
  - `min_per_variant: 3` (align with spec §3.1 review guideline)
  - `per_part_per_variant: 3` (soft budget multiplier)
  - `max_per_component: 40` (hard ceiling)
- **Formula:** For FocusScope with 1 Part and 2 States: budget = min(1×2×3, 40) = **6 snapshots minimum**
- **Files checked:** All `.snap` files in `crates/*/snapshots/` directories

### Critical Details for FocusScope Implementation

1. **Module location:** `/crates/ars-components/src/utility/focus_scope/mod.rs` (no separate files)
2. **Part enum** (from spec lines 38–50): Will have at least `Part::Root` + possibly `Part::Focusable` or context-specific parts
3. **State enum** (from spec lines 18–25): `State::Inactive` and `State::Active { trapped: bool }`
4. **Effects:** Will need transition effects for focus movement, timing, etc. — name them with an `Effect` enum variant and `PendingEffect::named(...)`
5. **Inline tests:** Add both unit tests (assertions) and snapshot tests in `mod.rs`; expect 6–10 snapshot tests based on the spec's 7 event types and 2 states
6. **No child modules:** Keep everything in single `mod.rs` file per workspace convention

File paths with line numbers for reference:
- **Dismissable patterns:** `/crates/ars-components/src/utility/dismissable/mod.rs` lines 1–1100+
- **Live Region (best Machine reference):** `/crates/ars-components/src/utility/live_region/mod.rs` lines 1–600+
- **Snapshot configuration:** `/xtask/src/ci/mod.rs` lines 619–644
- **Spec section:** `/spec/components/utility/focus-scope.md` §1 (State Machine), §1.3 (Context)

180	/// Typed metadata attached to a pending effect.
181	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]

201	/// A named side effect produced by a state transition.
202	///
203	/// Pending effects are returned from [`Service::send`] inside [`SendResult`].
204	/// The framework adapter is responsible for executing them by calling
205	/// [`run`](PendingEffect::run).
206	///
207	/// The setup function receives context, props, and a strong send handle.
208	/// It returns a [`CleanupFn`] invoked when the effect must stop (state
209	/// change or unmount).
210	pub struct PendingEffect<M: Machine> {
211	    /// Typed identifier for this effect intent. Adapters that dispatch on
212	    /// names typically write `match self.name { Effect::Foo => … }`
213	    /// exhaustively. The associated [`Machine::Effect`] type is required to
214	    /// be `Copy + Debug + Eq + Hash + Send + Sync + 'static`, which means
215	    /// adapters can also use it directly as a `HashMap` key for cleanup
216	    /// bookkeeping.
217	    pub name: M::Effect,
218	
219	    /// The state after the transition that produced this effect.
220	    /// Set by [`Service::drain_queue`] before returning to the adapter.
221	    pub target_state: Option<M::State>,
222	
223	    /// Optional typed metadata for adapters that need structured effect inputs.
224	    pub metadata: Option<EffectMetadata>,
225	
226	    /// Setup closure — receives a snapshot of context, props, and the
227	    /// strong send handle. Returns a cleanup function.
228	    pub(crate) setup: EffectSetupFn<M>,
229	}
230	
231	impl<M: Machine> PendingEffect<M> {
232	    /// Creates a new pending effect with a weak-send setup closure.
233	    ///
234	    /// The `setup` closure receives [`WeakSend`] (not the strong handle) to
235	    /// prevent retain cycles. `PendingEffect::new` bridges the strong→weak
236	    /// conversion internally.
237	    #[must_use]
238	    pub fn new(
239	        name: M::Effect,
240	        setup: impl FnOnce(&M::Context, &M::Props, WeakSend<M::Event>) -> CleanupFn + 'static,
241	    ) -> Self {
242	        Self {
243	            name,
244	            target_state: None,
245	            metadata: None,
246	            setup: Box::new(move |ctx, props, send: StrongSend<M::Event>| {
247	                let weak_send = WeakSend::from(&send);
248	                setup(ctx, props, weak_send)
249	            }),
250	        }
251	    }
252	
253	    /// Creates a marker-only effect with no-op setup.
254	    ///
255	    /// Useful when the effect name is the entire contract (the adapter
256	    /// implements the behavior based on the name alone).
257	    #[must_use]
258	    pub fn named(name: M::Effect) -> Self {
259	        Self {
260	            name,
261	            target_state: None,
262	            metadata: None,
263	            setup: Box::new(|_ctx, _props, _send| no_cleanup()),
264	        }
265	    }
266	
267	    /// Creates a marker-only effect with typed metadata.
268	    #[must_use]
269	    pub fn named_with_metadata(name: M::Effect, metadata: EffectMetadata) -> Self {
270	        Self {
271	            name,
272	            target_state: None,
273	            metadata: Some(metadata),
274	            setup: Box::new(|_ctx, _props, _send| no_cleanup()),
275	        }
276	    }
277	
278	    /// Execute the effect setup, consuming it.
279	    ///
280	    /// Called by the adapter after `Service::send()` returns. The adapter
281	    /// passes the current context snapshot, props, and its strong send handle.
282	    pub fn run(self, ctx: &M::Context, props: &M::Props, send: StrongSend<M::Event>) -> CleanupFn {
283	        (self.setup)(ctx, props, send)
284	    }
285	}
286	
287	impl<M: Machine> Debug for PendingEffect<M> {
288	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
289	        f.debug_struct("PendingEffect")
290	            .field("name", &self.name)
291	            .field("target_state", &self.target_state)
292	            .field("metadata", &self.metadata)
293	            .field("setup", &"<closure>")
294	            .finish()
295	    }
296	}

//! Dismissable behavior configuration and dismiss-button attributes.
//!
//! This module defines the adapter-facing data model for dismissable overlay
//! behavior — the props, component parts, and
//! [`dismiss_button_attrs`] function used by overlay components (`Dialog`,
//! `Popover`, `Tooltip`, `Select`, etc.) to produce consistent click-outside
//! and Escape-to-close behavior.
//!
//! The module intentionally stays free of DOM or framework types so that
//! attribute generation can be tested with pure unit tests.
//!
//! **Dismiss-button wording is not part of [`Props`].** [`Messages`] provides
//! the shared default label bundle, but callers resolve the localized label in
//! their adapter or overlay layer and pass the final string to
//! [`dismiss_button_attrs`]. This keeps the shared utility focused on behavior
//! and structure rather than resolving i18n itself.

use alloc::{string::String, sync::Arc, vec::Vec};
use core::{
    fmt::{self, Debug},
    sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering},
};

use ars_core::{
    AriaAttr, AttrMap, AttrValue, Callback, ComponentMessages, ComponentPart, ConnectApi, HtmlAttr,
    MessageFn,
};
use ars_i18n::Locale;
use ars_interactions::InteractOutsideEvent;

// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// DismissReason
// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/// Why a dismissable surface was dismissed.
///
/// Passed to [`Props::on_dismiss`] after the dismiss decision is finalized.
/// Per `spec/components/utility/dismissable.md` §11 "Callback Payload
/// Contract", the reason taxonomy carries the path that triggered the
/// dismissal so consumers (analytics, undo banners, …) can react differently
/// per source without re-implementing detection.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum DismissReason {
    /// A pointer event landed outside the dismissable surface and outside
    /// every registered inside-boundary or portal-owner.
    OutsidePointer,

    /// Focus moved to an element outside the dismissable surface and
    /// outside every registered inside-boundary or portal-owner.
    OutsideFocus,

    /// The user pressed the `Escape` key while the dismissable was the
    /// topmost overlay.
    Escape,

    /// The user activated one of the visually-hidden dismiss buttons (or a
    /// wrapper invoked the programmatic adapter handle's `dismiss`,
    /// e.g. `ars_leptos::utility::dismissable::Handle::dismiss`).
    DismissButton,
}

// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// DismissAttempt
// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/// Veto-capable wrapper passed to the dismiss-decision callbacks
/// (`on_interact_outside`, `on_escape_key_down`).
///
/// Per `spec/components/utility/dismissable.md` §11, those callbacks fire
/// **before** the final dismiss decision and may cancel it. Calling
/// [`prevent_dismiss`](Self::prevent_dismiss) sets a shared flag that the
/// adapter checks before dispatching `on_dismiss`. The flag is backed by an
/// [`Arc<AtomicBool>`] so the consumer's callback observation is visible to
/// the adapter regardless of thread of origin.
///
/// `event` is the underlying payload (e.g. [`InteractOutsideEvent`] for
/// outside-interaction callbacks, `()` for Escape).
pub struct DismissAttempt<E> {
    /// Underlying event payload.
    pub event: E,
    veto: Arc<AtomicBool>,
}

impl<E> DismissAttempt<E> {
    /// Creates a fresh dismiss attempt that is initially not vetoed.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn new(event: E) -> Self {
        Self {
            event,
            veto: Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false)),
        }
    }

    /// Marks the dismissal attempt as vetoed.
    ///
    /// After this call the adapter will skip `on_dismiss` for this event.
    /// Idempotent — calling repeatedly has no additional effect.
    pub fn prevent_dismiss(&self) {
        self.veto.store(true, Ordering::SeqCst);
    }

    /// Returns whether [`prevent_dismiss`](Self::prevent_dismiss) has been
    /// called for this attempt.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn is_prevented(&self) -> bool {
        self.veto.load(Ordering::SeqCst)
    }
}

impl<E: Clone> Clone for DismissAttempt<E> {
    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
        Self {
            event: self.event.clone(),
            veto: Arc::clone(&self.veto),
        }
    }
}

impl<E: Debug> Debug for DismissAttempt<E> {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
```

---

# Architectural Assessment

Your overall shape is correct. Confirmations and corrections inline.

## Confirmation of the approach

- Module location, public types, builder-style Props, named `Effect` enum, and the `Part`/`Container` connect API all line up with the established pattern (compare `crates/ars-components/src/utility/live_region/mod.rs:193-198` for `Effect` derives and `crates/ars-components/src/overlay/popover/mod.rs:95-96` for the `Hash`-derived enum used as a `HashMap` key in adapters).
- The `Machine::Effect` bound requires `Copy + Debug + Eq + Hash + Send + Sync + 'static` (`crates/ars-core/src/lib.rs:148`). Your derives match.
- Order of operations within `drain_queue` (`crates/ars-core/src/lib.rs:1248-1306`): **apply → then_send queued → state set → effects collected**. Effect **setup closures do not execute here at all** — they are returned to the adapter inside `SendResult.pending_effects` and the adapter calls `PendingEffect::run(&ctx, &props, send)` later with the current `ctx` snapshot.

## Resolution of your 5 questions

### Q1 — Order of operations for RestoreFocus

Your reading is right that `apply` mutates context **before** the effect is collected, but the relevant timing question is different. Effects are not run in `drain_queue`; they are run in the adapter after `Service::send` returns (`PendingEffect::run` at `crates/ars-core/src/lib.rs:282`). By then, `apply` has long since cleared `ctx.saved_focus`. So options (a)–(d) all need re-evaluation. The right answer is **(a) modeled on Dialog**:

Dialog never stores `saved_focus` in core context at all (it has no such field; see `crates/ars-components/src/overlay/dialog/mod.rs` — only `restore_focus: bool` in context). The adapter captures the trigger element when it dispatches `Event::Open`, stashes a native handle in adapter-scoped state, and reads that handle when `Effect::RestoreFocus` runs.

For FocusScope, the simplest and most consistent fix is:

- **Keep `ctx.saved_focus: Option<String>`** as the agnostic-core representation (the issue specifically says ID-only is fine if the adapter also has a richer handle).
- **In `Active + Deactivate { restore_focus: true }`**, emit `PendingEffect::named(Effect::RestoreFocus)` and **do NOT clear `ctx.saved_focus` in this plan's `apply`**. Leave the value in place.
- **Clear `ctx.saved_focus` on the next `Activate`** (the `Inactive → Active` plan's apply overwrites it with the fresh `saved_focus_id`).
- **Drop the separate `Event::RestoreFocus`** entirely. It is unreachable as documented (only fires after `Deactivate`, only when `Inactive`, no test in the issue exercises it) and removing it eliminates the ordering problem.

Effect of this resolution: stale-token concern becomes a non-issue because (i) the adapter validates via `can_restore_focus` before focusing, and (ii) the only other way to clear it is to call `Activate` again, which overwrites it. This is exactly what Dialog does in spirit.

If you want belt-and-suspenders, you can also extend `EffectMetadata` to add `RestoreFocus { target_id: Option<String>, container_id: Option<String> }` (the enum already has one tuple variant — `ResizeToContent` at `crates/ars-core/src/lib.rs:182-185`), and emit the metadata at plan-build time using `PendingEffect::named_with_metadata`. But for FocusScope alone, this is over-engineering; only do it if other components also need to bundle IDs into effects.

### Q2 — Remove `get_tabbable_elements()` from the Api

**Remove it.** The issue explicitly says "tabbable queries, and active-element validation must remain adapter/platform effects." A stub returning `vec![]` would be a silent-contract violation and creates a false API surface in the agnostic core. The adapter Api wrapper (Leptos/Dioxus) can expose `tabbable_element_ids()` directly via `PlatformEffects::tabbable_element_ids` — that is the right layer for live DOM queries. Update spec §1.7 to drop this method from the core Api.

### Q3 — `initial_effects` and the lack of `default_active`

Skip the `initial_effects` override entirely. The current spec exposes no `default_active`/`default_trapped` prop, and `init` always returns `State::Inactive` (spec §1.6 lines 202-209). With no path to boot active, `initial_effects` would be dead code. Dialog and Popover only override it because they have `default_open`. This is also why your Q3 hesitation about adding `default_active` is the right instinct: adding props is out of scope for this task. The issue lists only `trap`, `auto_focus`, `restore_focus`, `contain` — leave `initial_effects` to the trait default (returns empty `Vec`).

If a future task adds `default_active`, that PR can add the override.

### Q4 — Should `Messages` derive `Eq`?

Either is fine. The trait bound is `ComponentMessages + Clone + Default + 'static` (`crates/ars-core/src/lib.rs:744`) — no `Eq`. Match `live_region`'s style (`#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)] pub struct Messages;`) for an empty struct — it makes test assertions and snapshot equality work without surprises and costs nothing.

### Q5 — Other spec drift

I found four additional drift points, plus your three:

1. **Spec §1.6 line 202 `init` signature** uses unqualified `Env` but the trait import path is `ars_core::Env`. Minor — adapters compile fine either way. Not blocking.
2. **Spec §1.6 line 228** wires `with_named_effect("focus-trap-listener", ...)` where `M::Effect` is required to be `Copy + Eq + Hash`. The name `"focus-trap-listener"` is a `&'static str`, not your `Effect` enum variant. This is part of the same drift you flagged — fix by replacing every `with_named_effect("...", |...| { use_platform_effects() })` with `with_effect(PendingEffect::named(Effect::X))`.
3. **Spec §1.6.1 `restore_focus_safely` function** must move out of the core spec entirely. The fallback-chain logic is an **adapter contract**, not core code. Replace the fenced Rust block with a narrative bullet list under §7 "Focus Restoration Fallbacks" (which already documents the chain in prose). The function signature can live in spec/foundation/11-dom-utilities.md §3 if the team wants a normative reference.
4. **Spec §1.6 line 231 `platform.attach_focus_trap(container_id, on_escape)`**: the trait at `crates/ars-core/src/platform.rs:118` is `fn attach_focus_trap(&self, container_id: &str, on_escape: Box<dyn Fn()>) -> Box<dyn FnOnce()>`. The return type (`Box<dyn FnOnce()>`) is the cleanup closure the adapter wires into the effect's `CleanupFn`. Spec should note this.
5. **Issue #212 vs spec attribute name**: spec uses `data-ars-trapped` (line 446) and issue test 9 uses `data-ars-focus-trapped`. **Match the spec** (your call is correct). Add a comment in the test naming both for cross-reference, and resolve in the PR description.
6. **Spec §3 line 554**: "tabindex='-1' is set on the container when active" — implementation should set this only on `Active` (matches your plan).

## Test list adjustments

Issue #212 lists 10 tests. With Effect-marker resolution, two tests need rewording:

- Test 5/6 (Tab wrap intent): in the agnostic core, "Tab from last requests wrap to first" is **not testable** because Tab handling is in the adapter. Reformulate as: "`FocusFirst` event emits `Effect::FocusFirst` while `Active`" and "`FocusLast` event emits `Effect::FocusLast` while `Active`". The acceptance criterion "Focus trapping intent is emitted" is satisfied by asserting `Effect::FocusTrapListener` appears in `pending_effects` after `Activate`.
- Test 7 (restoration intent without DOM access): assert `Effect::RestoreFocus` is in `pending_effects` after `Deactivate { restore_focus: true }`, and absent when `restore_focus: false`.
- Test 10 (sentinel/keydown effects described): assert `Effect::FocusTrapListener` is in `pending_effects`. The sentinel installation is an adapter detail; the core only emits the marker.

Add these snapshot tests (mirroring live_region's pattern at `mod.rs:1034-1080`): `container_attrs_inactive`, `container_attrs_active_not_trapped`, `container_attrs_active_trapped`. Three `.snap` files committed.
