# FocusScope — Leptos Adapter

## 1. Purpose and Adapter Scope

This spec maps the core [`FocusScope`](../../components/utility/focus-scope.md) machine to Leptos 0.8.x.

## 2. Public Adapter API

```rust,no_check
#[component] pub fn FocusScope(...) -> impl IntoView
```

The adapter exposes the full core focus-scope prop set for trapping, looping, autofocus, and restore-focus behavior.

## 3. Mapping to Core Component Contract

- Props parity: full parity.
- Event parity: focus containment and restoration are adapter-driven on top of the core machine.

## 4. Part Mapping

| Core part / structure      | Required?                | Adapter rendering target                   | Ownership      | Attr source                                    | Notes                                        |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------ | -------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
| `Container`                | required                 | wrapper `<div>` or any chosen root element | adapter-owned  | `api.part_attrs(focus_scope::Part::Container)` | Focus trap boundary.                         |
| trapped descendants region | required structural node | consumer children inside container         | consumer-owned | none                                           | Focus stays within this region while active. |

## 5. Attr Merge and Ownership Rules

| Target node           | Core attrs                                 | Adapter-owned attrs                                     | Consumer attrs      | Merge order                                                     | Ownership notes                                         |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Container`           | `api.part_attrs(Part::Container)`          | focus-sentinel or guard attrs if the strategy uses them | consumer root attrs | core focus-boundary attrs win; `class`/`style` merge additively | adapter-owned scope container                           |
| helper nodes (if any) | none from core unless modeled as structure | sentinel, guard, or helper attrs                        | none                | adapter attrs apply directly                                    | helper nodes are adapter-owned and never consumer-owned |

## 6. Composition / Context Contract

May compose with `ArsProvider` for scoped DOM queries and with `Dismissable` inside the same container.
When an adapter chooses to expose programmatic navigation, it should publish a `FocusManager`-style context with `focus_next`, `focus_previous`, `focus_first`, and `focus_last` helpers for descendants that need imperative focus movement without bypassing the scope contract.

## 7. Prop Sync and Event Mapping

Focus-scope behavior is mostly interaction-driven. Configuration props are usually init-only unless a wrapper explicitly reinitializes the scope.

50	
51	| Adapter prop                        | Mode                      | Sync trigger     | Machine event / update path | Visible effect                               | Notes                                               |
52	| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ---------------- | --------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
53	| trap / loop / restore configuration | non-reactive adapter prop | render time only | initial machine props       | determines trapping and restoration behavior | dynamic changes require documented reinitialization |
54	
55	| UI event            | Preconditions                           | Machine event / callback path | Ordering notes                                   | Notes                                               |
56	| ------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------- |
57	| initial activation  | client-side mount with trapping enabled | scope-activation path         | activation occurs after the container ref exists | no SSR focus work                                   |
58	| `Tab` / `Shift+Tab` | active scope and trapping enabled       | normalized focus-wrap path    | scope registry lookup precedes focus movement    | keeps focus inside the container                    |
59	| cleanup / unmount   | scope active                            | restore-focus path            | nested scopes unwind in stack order              | restore fallback applies if prior target is missing |
60	
61	## 8. Registration and Cleanup Contract
62	
63	- Each active focus scope registers in a scope stack on mount.
64	- Nested scopes must unwind in last-in, first-out order.
65	- Helper nodes or sentinels, if used, are owned by the scope instance and cleaned up before focus restoration completes.
66	
67	| Registered entity        | Registration trigger                    | Identity key                        | Cleanup trigger | Cleanup action                                 | Notes               |
68	| ------------------------ | --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | --------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
69	| active scope stack entry | client-side mount with scope activation | scope instance                      | scope cleanup   | remove stack entry and run restore-focus logic | no SSR registration |
70	| helper nodes / sentinels | helper-node render                      | scope instance plus helper identity | scope cleanup   | remove helper nodes and drop refs              | optional strategy   |
71	
72	## 9. Ref and Node Contract
73	
74	| Target part / node                  | Ref required?                           | Ref owner                      | Node availability    | Composition rule                                                    | Notes                                                                            |
75	| ----------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
76	| `Container`                         | yes                                     | adapter-owned                  | required after mount | compose only when wrappers expose the scope container               | The trap boundary requires a concrete node.                                      |
77	| helper nodes or sentinels when used | yes                                     | adapter-owned                  | client-only          | no consumer composition unless the strategy explicitly exposes them | These handles are part of the trap implementation, not consumer-owned structure. |
78	| restore-focus target                | yes if a previously focused node exists | adapter-owned transient handle | client-only          | not compositional                                                   | Stored as a transient node handle, not a rendered part.                          |
79	
80	## 10. State Machine Boundary Rules
81	
82	- machine-owned state: active scope status, nesting behavior, trap/loop semantics, and restore-focus decision rules.
83	- adapter-local derived bookkeeping: transient previous-focus handle, helper-node refs, and active-scope stack handles.
84	- forbidden local mirrors: do not fork a parallel active/inactive scope flag separate from the machine/scope stack.
85	- allowed snapshot-read contexts: mount activation effects, Tab handlers, restore-focus cleanup, and render derivation.
86	
87	## 11. Callback Payload Contract
88	
89	| Callback                                                                     | Payload source | Payload shape | Timing         | Cancelable? | Notes                                                                                          |
90	| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------------- | -------------- | ----------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
91	| no public adapter-specific callback unless a wrapper exposes lifecycle hooks | none           | none          | not applicable | no          | Focus restoration and trap behavior remain internal unless a wrapper explicitly surfaces them. |
92	
93	## 12. Failure and Degradation Rules
94	
95	| Condition                                | Policy             | Notes                                                                    |
96	| ---------------------------------------- | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
97	| scope container ref missing after mount  | fail fast          | Focus trapping requires the container node.                              |
98	| previous focus target missing on restore | degrade gracefully | Fall back to the documented restore target behavior instead of erroring. |
99	| browser focus APIs absent during SSR     | no-op              | Render the structure and defer focus logic to the client.                |
100	
101	## 13. Identity and Key Policy
102	
103	| Registered or repeated structure | Identity source  | Duplicates allowed?   | DOM order must match registration order? | SSR/hydration stability                             | Notes                                        |
104	| -------------------------------- | ---------------- | --------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
105	| active scope stack entry         | instance-derived | yes for nested scopes | stack order must match activation order  | scope structure must remain stable across hydration | Nested scopes are keyed by scope instance.   |
106	| helper-node identities           | composite        | yes                   | not applicable                           | helper nodes are client-only                        | Identity is scope instance plus helper role. |
107	
108	## 14. SSR and Client Boundary Rules
109	
110	- SSR must render the scope container and trapped descendants region.
111	- Scope activation, helper-node setup, and restore-focus logic are client-only.
112	- Container/helper refs are server-safe absent and required after mount.
113	- Nested-scope structural ownership must remain consistent across hydration.
114	
115	## 15. Performance Constraints
116	
117	- Active-scope registration must only change on mount/unmount or activation changes, not every render.
118	- Helper-node setup should be instance-scoped and must not churn when the trapped children rerender.
119	- Restore-focus cleanup should run once per scope cleanup and should not scan the DOM repeatedly when a stored target exists.
120	
121	## 16. Implementation Dependencies
122	
123	| Dependency     | Required?   | Dependency type         | Why it must exist first                                                   | Notes                                         |
124	| -------------- | ----------- | ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
125	| `ars-provider` | recommended | context contract        | Scoped DOM access can simplify focus queries in complex trees.            | Helpful for advanced composed consumers.      |
126	| `dismissable`  | recommended | behavioral prerequisite | Overlay shells often combine focus trapping with dismissable containment. | Not required to implement the utility itself. |
127	
128	## 17. Recommended Implementation Sequence
129	
130	1. Establish the scope container ref and any helper-node strategy.
131	2. Activate the scope on mount using the documented machine rules.
132	3. Publish optional `FocusManager` context after the scope boundary and activation path are stable.
133	4. Wire Tab/Shift+Tab trapping and nested-scope stack behavior.
134	5. Add restore-focus cleanup and fallback behavior.
135	6. Verify client-only activation, optional context publication, and cleanup test oracles.
136	
137	## 18. Anti-Patterns
138	
139	- Do not activate focus trapping during SSR.
140	- Do not store restore targets as IDs only when the contract requires a live node handle.
141	- Do not let nested scopes unwind out of stack order.
142	
143	## 19. Consumer Expectations and Guarantees
144	
145	- Consumers may assume focus trapping and restore-focus ordering follow the active scope stack.
146	- Consumers may assume optional focus-manager publication, when exposed, already respects the current scope boundary.
147	- Consumers must not assume focus logic runs during SSR or before the container ref exists.
148	
149	## 20. Platform Support Matrix
150	
151	| Capability / behavior              | Browser client | SSR            | Notes                                                            |
152	| ---------------------------------- | -------------- | -------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
153	| focus trapping and restore         | full support   | client-only    | SSR renders structure only.                                      |
154	| optional focus-manager publication | full support   | SSR-safe empty | Context may publish without live focus side effects until mount. |
155	
156	## 21. Debug Diagnostics and Production Policy
157	
158	| Condition                                                           | Debug build behavior | Production behavior | Notes                                                                        |
159	| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
160	| documented platform capability is unavailable on the active runtime | debug warning        | degrade gracefully  | Use the documented fallback path instead of inventing browser-only behavior. |
161	
162	## 22. Shared Adapter Helper Notes
163	
164	| Helper concept                    | Required?   | Responsibility                                                                                               | Reused by                                 | Notes                                                         |
165	| --------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
166	| focus-manager publication helper  | recommended | Publish `focus_next`, `focus_previous`, `focus_first`, and `focus_last` without leaking raw focus internals. | `focus-scope`, composed overlay utilities | Only needed when the adapter exposes programmatic navigation. |
167	| node-boundary registration helper | required    | Track the active scope container, helper nodes, and restore target handles.                                  | `focus-scope`, `dismissable`              | Focus restoration depends on live-node ownership.             |
168	
169	## 23. Framework-Specific Behavior
170	
171	Leptos uses `on_cleanup` for focus restoration cleanup. When programmatic navigation is exposed, the adapter should publish a `FocusManager` through `provide_context(FocusManager { ... })` after the container ref and scope activation path exist.
172	
173	## 24. Canonical Implementation Sketch
174	
175	```rust
176	#[component]
177	pub fn FocusScope(children: Children) -> impl IntoView {
178	    let machine = use_machine::<focus_scope::Machine>(focus_scope::Props::default());
179	    let attrs = machine.derive(|api| api.part_attrs(focus_scope::Part::Container));
180	    view! { <div {..attrs.get()}>{children()}</div> }
181	}
182	```
183	
184	## 25. Reference Implementation Skeleton
185	
186	```rust,no_check
187	let machine = use_machine::<focus_scope::Machine>(props);
188	let container_ref = create_container_ref();
189	let scope_stack = create_scope_registration_helper();
190	let restore_target = store_previous_focus_handle();
191	let focus_manager = maybe_publish_focus_manager(machine, container_ref);
192	
193	render_scope_container_and_optional_helper_nodes(container_ref);
194	activate_scope_on_mount(scope_stack, container_ref);
195	wire_tab_looping_and_nested_scope_order(machine, scope_stack);
196	
197	on_cleanup(|| {
198	    scope_stack.unregister();
199	    restore_focus_if_needed(restore_target);
200	    release_helper_nodes();
201	});
202	```
203	
204	## 26. Adapter Invariants
205	
206	- All trapped descendants must remain inside the owned scope container used as the focus boundary.
207	- Nested scope activation and restore-focus ordering must remain deterministic.
208	- Restore-focus fallback behavior must be explicit when the previously focused element no longer exists.
209	- Focus sentinels, guards, or helper dismiss controls must remain inside the trapped structure whenever they are rendered.
210	- Client-only focus trapping logic must not execute during SSR.
211	
212	## 27. Accessibility and SSR Notes
213	
214	Trap behavior is client-only, but the container structure must still be documented and rendered consistently.
215	
216	## 28. Parity Summary and Intentional Deviations
217	
218	Parity summary: full core prop, part, and lifecycle parity.
219	
220	Intentional deviations: none.
221	
222	Traceability note: This adapter spec now makes explicit the core adapter-owned concerns for live restore-target validation, nested scope ordering, client-only trap activation, helper-node ownership, and optional focus-manager publication.
223	
224	## 29. Test Scenarios
225	
226	- container mapping
227	- trap activation
228	- nested scope behavior
229	- focus restore fallback
230	- descendants can access focus navigation helpers through context when the adapter exposes them
231	
232	## 30. Test Oracle Notes
233	
234	| Behavior                           | Preferred oracle type | Notes                                                                                             |
235	| ---------------------------------- | --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
236	| nested scope stack behavior        | machine state         | Assert active-scope stack order and restoration policy.                                           |
237	| focus restoration                  | cleanup side effects  | Verify focus lands on the documented restore target or fallback after cleanup.                    |
238	| scope container/helper structure   | rendered structure    | Assert the container and any helper nodes remain correctly placed.                                |
239	| optional focus-manager publication | context registration  | Verify descendants can read the published focus navigation helpers when the adapter exposes them. |
240	
241	Cheap verification recipe:
242	
243	1. Render a scope with a focusable child, activate trapping on the client, and assert the container plus helper structure before moving focus.
244	2. Open nested scopes, then close them in reverse order and verify focus restores to the documented live target or fallback.
245	3. If the adapter publishes `FocusManager`, read it from a descendant and verify the context handle exists without using it as proof of trap activation by itself.
246	
247	## 31. Implementation Checklist
248	
249	- [ ] Container ref and any helper-node refs exist before trap activation.
250	- [ ] Nested scope activation and restore order match the documented rules.
251	- [ ] Restore-focus fallback behavior is verified.
252	- [ ] Optional `FocusManager` publication is documented and verified when the adapter exposes programmatic navigation.
253	- [ ] Cleanup and test oracles cover focus restoration and structure.
254	
```

246	
247	## 14. Implementation Roadmap
248	
249	### 14.1 Phase 1: P0 Foundation (25 components including 4 a11y utilities)
250	
251	Core infrastructure + foundational components:
252	
253	1. `ars-core`: Machine trait, Service, Connect (ConnectApi trait), AttrMap, Bindable
254	2. `ars-a11y`: ARIA types, FocusScope, FocusRing, LiveRegion, VisuallyHidden (these are also public P0 components counted in the 25 total)
255	3. `ars-dom`: ID generation, focus utilities, positioning engine
256	4. `ars-interactions`: Press, Hover, Focus (basics)
257	5. Components: Button, ToggleButton, Checkbox, CheckboxGroup, RadioGroup, Switch, TextField, Textarea, Link
258	6. Components: Dialog, Popover, Tabs, Select, Menu, ContextMenu
259	7. Utilities: Presence, Portal, Dismissable, AsChild, ArsProvider, Form
260	8. Adapters: ars-leptos, ars-dioxus (basic wiring)
261	
262	### 14.2 Phase 2: P1 Essential (32 components/utilities)
263	
264	Common patterns + i18n foundation:
265	
266	1. `ars-i18n`: Locale system, RTL, number formatting
267	2. `ars-collections`: Collection trait, selection model
268	3. `ars-forms`: Validation framework, field association
269	4. Components: NumberInput, Slider, RangeSlider, SearchInput, Combobox, Listbox
270	5. Components: Tooltip, Toast, AlertDialog, Drawer, Accordion, Collapsible
```

92	/// Hydration-safe `FocusScope` setup for modal overlays.
93	#[cfg(all(feature = "hydrate", target_arch = "wasm32"))]
94	pub fn setup_focus_scope_hydration_safe(
95	    scope_id: String,
96	    restore_target: StoredValue<Option<web_sys::HtmlElement>, LocalStorage>,
97	) {
98	    let cleanup_scope_id = scope_id.clone();
99	    let activation_active = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(true));
100	
101	    Effect::new({
102	        let activation_active = Arc::clone(&activation_active);
103	
104	        move |_| {
105	            let Some(document) = browser_document() else {
106	                return;
107	            };
108	
109	            if body_is_hydrated(&document) {
110	                activate_focus_scope(
111	                    &document,
112	                    &scope_id,
113	                    restore_target,
114	                    Arc::clone(&activation_active),
115	                );
116	            } else {
117	                request_hydration_activation_after_frame(
118	                    scope_id.clone(),
119	                    restore_target,
120	                    Arc::clone(&activation_active),
121	                );
122	            }
123	        }
124	    });
125	
126	    on_cleanup(move || {
127	        activation_active.store(false, Ordering::Relaxed);
128	
129	        if let Some(document) = browser_document()
130	            && let Some(scope) = document.get_element_by_id(&cleanup_scope_id)
131	        {
132	            drop(scope.remove_attribute("data-ars-modal-open"));
133	        }
134	
135	        restore_target.with_value(|element| {
136	            if let Some(element) = element {
137	                drop(element.focus());
138	            }
139	        });
140	    });
141	}
142	
143	#[cfg(all(feature = "hydrate", target_arch = "wasm32"))]
144	fn browser_document() -> Option<web_sys::Document> {
145	    web_sys::window()?.document()
146	}
147	
148	#[cfg(all(feature = "hydrate", target_arch = "wasm32"))]
149	fn body_is_hydrated(document: &web_sys::Document) -> bool {
150	    document
151	        .body()
152	        .and_then(|body| body.get_attribute("data-ars-hydrated"))
153	        .is_some()
154	}
155	
156	#[cfg(all(feature = "hydrate", target_arch = "wasm32"))]
157	fn activate_focus_scope(
158	    document: &web_sys::Document,
159	    scope_id: &str,

> AGENT

Implementation complete. Here is the consolidated audit + verification summary for your review.

## Summary

**Task #212 — Implement FocusScope agnostic core**

### Files

- **New** `crates/ars-components/src/utility/focus_scope/mod.rs` (~1325 LOC, including 31 unit + connect-API tests, 4 snapshot tests, 1 spec-conformance regression test)
- **New** `crates/ars-components/src/utility/focus_scope/snapshots/` (4 committed `.snap` fixtures)
- **Modified** `crates/ars-components/src/utility/mod.rs` (registered `pub mod focus_scope`)
- **Modified** `crates/ars-components/tests/proptest_state_machines/utility.rs` (3 proptest invariants + arbitraries)
- **Modified** `crates/ars-components/tests/spec_conformance/utility.rs` (6 conformance tests, including the `data-ars-trapped` vs `data-ars-focus-trapped` issue-typo regression guard)
- **Modified** `spec/components/utility/focus-scope.md` — substantial sync (§1.2–§1.8): named-effect contract, `Effect` enum, removed adapter helpers from core block, updated Api signatures, added accessors/Debug/`AcceptFn`

### Audit findings (all landed in this PR)

**Phase 1 (spec/impl drift):** 11 spec-only updates resolving the original `use_platform_effects()` drift plus 10 minor catch-ups where the impl made better choices than the spec sketched (builder methods, `const fn`, `&FocusNavigationOptions` by reference, `Eq`/`Default` derives, accessors, manual `Debug`, `AcceptFn` alias).

**Phase 2 (round 1 + round 2):** No new in-scope findings. Pre-existing items intentionally deferred: (a) `spec/foundation/02-component-catalog.md:254` still lists FocusScope/FocusRing/LiveRegion/VisuallyHidden in `ars-a11y` — actual placement is `ars-components::utility::*`; this misnames four components, not just FocusScope, so it's a workspace-cleanup task. (b) `ars-leptos::hydration::setup_focus_scope_hydration_safe` is a Leptos-specific hydration helper that operates independently of the new agnostic-core surface — it's task #196's territory.

**Phase 3 (test coverage):**
| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Lines | 97.76% (656/671) — uncovered are `&\|_\| {}` no-op test closures (CLAUDE.md exception) |
| Regions | 98.59% |
| Mutations | 24 caught / 0 missed (100% on viable mutants; 11 unviable) |
| Unit + connect-API tests | 31 |
| Snapshot tests | 4 (all variant × part combinations) |
| Proptest invariants | 3 |
| Spec-conformance tests | 6 |

### Verification gates

```
cargo nextest run -p ars-components             →  2295 tests run, 2295 passed
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets …        →  no warnings
cargo insta test -p ars-components --unreferenced=reject --all-features
                                                 →  no unreferenced snapshots
cargo xtask spec validate                        →  340 files validated
cargo xtask ci snapshot-count                    →  passed
cargo xtask ci spec-compile-snippets             →  2173 blocks parsed, no findings
cargo mutants -p ars-components -f .../focus_scope/mod.rs
                                                 →  24 caught, 11 unviable, 0 missed
```

### Issue/spec note

GitHub issue #212 test 9 says `data-ars-focus-trapped`; spec §2 says `data-ars-trapped`. The spec is authoritative. The impl emits `data-ars-trapped` and `tests::container_attrs_never_emits_data_ars_focus_trapped_typo` plus `tests::focus_scope_connect_api_emits_data_ars_trapped_not_focus_trapped` (spec-conformance) pin the spec-correct name.

### Ready for next steps

Per CLAUDE.md workflow step 8, this is the user-review checkpoint. Once you approve, I will run `cargo xci --profile fast` and then commit + push as a PR closing #212.

1	//! `FocusScope` component state machine and connect API.
2	//!
3	//! `FocusScope` constrains keyboard Tab focus within a container, enabling
4	//! focus trapping for `Dialog`, `Drawer`, `Popover`, and other overlay
5	//! components. The agnostic core emits typed effect intents
6	//! ([`Effect`]) that adapters route through their `PlatformEffects`
7	//! implementation — the core never touches the DOM or framework helpers.
8	//!
9	//! See `spec/components/utility/focus-scope.md` for the full contract.

  "old_string": "// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// FocusNavigationOptions\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Reference-counted predicate filter used by [`FocusNavigationOptions::accept`].\n///\n/// Wrapping the trait object in [`Rc`] keeps [`FocusNavigationOptions`]\n/// trivially [`Clone`] while letting consumers share the predicate\n/// across multiple calls without re-allocating.\npub type AcceptFn = Rc<dyn Fn(&str) -> bool>;\n\n/// Options for adapter-provided focus navigation.\n///\n/// The agnostic core only carries the option payload; behavior lives in\n/// each adapter's platform-aware override of [`Api::focus_next`] and\n/// [`Api::focus_previous`].\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct FocusNavigationOptions {\n    /// Wrap around at boundaries.\n    pub wrap: bool,\n    /// Element ID to start from (default: currently focused element).\n    pub from: Option<String>,\n    /// Only consider tabbable elements (`tabindex >= 0`).\n    pub tabbable: bool,\n    /// Custom filter predicate that receives an element ID.\n    pub accept: Option<AcceptFn>,\n}\n\nimpl Debug for FocusNavigationOptions {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        f.debug_struct(\"FocusNavigationOptions\")\n            .field(\"wrap\", &self.wrap)\n            .field(\"from\", &self.from)\n            .field(\"tabbable\", &self.tabbable)\n            .field(\"accept\", &self.accept.as_ref().map(|_| \"<predicate>\"))\n            .finish()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for FocusNavigationOptions {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            wrap: false,\n            from: None,\n            tabbable: true,\n            accept: None,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Tests\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────",
  "new_string": "// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Tests\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────"
}

The alias also satisfies the\n/// workspace's `clippy::type_complexity` lint on the `accept` field.\npub type AcceptFn = Rc<dyn Fn(&str) -> bool>;\n\n/// Options for focus navigation.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct FocusNavigationOptions {\n    /// Wrap around at boundaries.\n    pub wrap: bool,\n    /// Element ID to start from (default: currently focused element).\n    pub from: Option<String>,\n    /// Only consider tabbable elements (tabindex >= 0).\n    pub tabbable: bool,\n    /// Custom filter predicate that receives an element ID.\n    pub accept: Option<AcceptFn>,\n}\n\n/// `accept` is a trait object that does not derive `Debug`, so a manual\n/// impl prints a stable `<predicate>` placeholder instead of leaking the\n/// closure's address.\nimpl Debug for FocusNavigationOptions {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        f.debug_struct(\"FocusNavigationOptions\")\n            .field(\"wrap\", &self.wrap)\n            .field(\"from\", &self.from)\n            .field(\"tabbable\", &self.tabbable)\n            .field(\"accept\", &self.accept.as_ref().map(|_| \"<predicate>\"))\n            .finish()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for FocusNavigationOptions {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            wrap: false,\n            from: None,\n            tabbable: true,\n            accept: None,\n        }\n    }\n}\n```",
  "new_string": "```\n\n**Programmatic sequential navigation lives in the adapter layer, not the\ncore.** `focus_next` / `focus_previous` and any `FocusNavigationOptions`-style\nconfig struct would require synchronous DOM lookups (tabbable filtering,\n`activeElement`, `accept` predicate evaluation against live element handles)\nthat the agnostic core cannot perform — the issue's \"Element/ref handling\nnote\" explicitly forbids ID-only or DOM-bound APIs at this layer. Adapters\nthat need this behavior (consumed by `Toolbar`, `ActionGroup`, `TreeView`,\nnested `FocusScope`s) expose it on their own API surface and publish a\n`FocusManager` context as described in §1.7 *Focus Manager Context* below;\nthe agnostic core only emits `Event::FocusFirst` / `Event::FocusLast` for the\ntwo end-of-list targets it can describe as effect intents."

557	
558	**Programmatic sequential navigation lives in the adapter layer, not the
559	core.** `focus_next` / `focus_previous` and any `FocusNavigationOptions`-style
560	config struct would require synchronous DOM lookups (tabbable filtering,
561	`activeElement`, `accept` predicate evaluation against live element handles)
562	that the agnostic core cannot perform — the issue's "Element/ref handling
563	note" explicitly forbids ID-only or DOM-bound APIs at this layer. Adapters
564	that need this behavior (consumed by `Toolbar`, `ActionGroup`, `TreeView`,
565	nested `FocusScope`s) expose it on their own API surface and publish a
566	`FocusManager` context as described in §1.7 *Focus Manager Context* below;
567	the agnostic core only emits `Event::FocusFirst` / `Event::FocusLast` for the
568	two end-of-list targets it can describe as effect intents.
569	
570	### 1.8 Effect Contract
571	
572	Adapters MUST provide a handler for every [`Effect`] variant. Each handler
573	receives `&Context`, `&Props`, and a `WeakSend<Event>` send handle, and is
574	expected to call into the workspace's `PlatformEffects` implementation
575	(see `spec/foundation/01-architecture.md` §2.2.7 and
576	`spec/foundation/11-dom-utilities.md` §3) rather than the DOM directly.
577	
578	| Effect              | When emitted                                                          | Adapter contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
579	| ------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
580	| `FocusTrapListener` | `Inactive → Active` (Activate)                                        | Call `PlatformEffects::attach_focus_trap(container_id, on_escape)`. Wire the returned `CleanupFn` as the effect's cleanup so `cancel_effect(Effect::FocusTrapListener)` tears the handler down on `Deactivate`. The `on_escape` callback SHOULD send `Event::Deactivate { restore_focus: true }`.        |
581	| `FocusFirst`        | `Active + FocusFirst` (also on `Activate` when `props.auto_focus`)    | Call `PlatformEffects::focus_first_tabbable(container_id)`. Fall back to focusing the container element (which has `tabindex="-1"` while active) when no tabbable descendants exist.                                                                                                                    |
582	| `FocusLast`         | `Active + FocusLast`                                                  | Call `PlatformEffects::focus_last_tabbable(container_id)`. Fall back to the container when no tabbable descendants exist.                                                                                                                                                                                |
583	| `RestoreFocus`      | `Active → Inactive` (Deactivate with `restore_focus: true`) or explicit `Event::RestoreFocus` while `Inactive` | Read `service.context().saved_focus`. If `Some(id)`, apply the §1.6.1 / §7 fallback chain via `PlatformEffects::can_restore_focus` → `focus_element_by_id` → `nearest_focusable_ancestor_id` → `focus_body`. The core does not clear `saved_focus` here; the next `Activate` overwrites it.            |
584	
585	#### Focus Manager Context
586	
587	The adapter SHOULD provide the FocusScope's navigation methods (`focus_next`, `focus_previous`, `focus_first`, `focus_last`) via framework context, allowing child components to programmatically manage focus without prop drilling. In Leptos: `provide_context(FocusManager { ... })`. In Dioxus: `use_context_provider(|| FocusManager { ... })`. This mirrors React Aria's `useFocusManager()` hook pattern.
588	
589	## 2. Anatomy
590	
591	```text
592	FocusScope
593	└── Container    <div> (or any element)    data-ars-scope="focus-scope"
594	                                           data-ars-part="container"
595	                                           data-ars-active (when active)
596	                                           data-ars-trapped (when trapped)
597	```
598	
599	| Part      | Element          | Key Attributes                                                                                     |
600	| --------- | ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
601	| Container | `<div>` (or any) | `data-ars-scope="focus-scope"`, `data-ars-part="container"`, `data-ars-active`, `data-ars-trapped` |
602	
603	## 3. Accessibility
604	
605	### 3.1 ARIA Roles, States, and Properties
606	
607	- No ARIA role is set on the container — FocusScope is a behavioral utility, not a semantic landmark.
608	- `tabindex="-1"` is set on the container when active, allowing programmatic focus when no tabbable children exist.
609	- `data-ars-active` and `data-ars-trapped` are data attributes for styling hooks; they are not ARIA attributes.
610	
611	### 3.2 Focus Management
612	
613	- A focus trap is required for modal dialogs per ARIA 1.2 (APG Modal Dialog pattern).
614	- The scope must include a way to close it reachable by keyboard (typically Escape key handled
615	  by the parent component, not by `FocusScope` itself).
616	- `auto_focus` moves focus into the dialog on open, which is required for screen reader users
617	  to know the dialog has appeared.
618	- `restore_focus` returns focus to the trigger on close, maintaining orientation in the page.
619	
620	## 4. Internationalization
621	
622	Label text is consumer-provided. `data-ars-*` attribute values are stable API tokens, not localized. RTL: no special handling needed — focus order follows DOM order regardless of text direction.
623	
624	## 5. Tabbable Element Detection
625	
626	The tabbable element query matches (in DOM order):
627	
628	```css
629	a[href]:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
630	button:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
631	input:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
632	select:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
633	textarea:not([tabindex="-1"]):not([disabled]),
634	[contenteditable]:not([tabindex="-1"]),
635	[tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])
636	```
637	
638	Elements with `visibility:hidden`, `display:none`, or inside a `<details>` (closed) are excluded.
639	
```

The expected handles are:\n\n- `focus_first` / `focus_last` — thin wrappers that call\n  [`Api::focus_first`] / [`Api::focus_last`], so the underlying transition\n  still flows through the agnostic core's `Effect::FocusFirst` /\n  `Effect::FocusLast` markers.\n- `focus_next` / `focus_previous` — **adapter-owned**; these resolve live\n  tabbable element handles via `PlatformEffects::tabbable_element_ids`,\n  `active_element_id`, and `focus_element_by_id`, plus any adapter-defined\n  `FocusNavigationOptions` (wrap, accept predicate, starting element, etc.).\n  They are intentionally absent from the agnostic-core [`Api`] surface so\n  that the workspace's \"no synchronous DOM lookups in the core\" rule stays\n  enforced.\n\nPublication: `provide_context(FocusManager { ... })` in Leptos,\n`use_context_provider(|| FocusManager { ... })` in Dioxus."

820	1. The saved focus target, if still valid in the DOM and focusable
821	2. The scope's trigger element (the element that caused the scope to activate)
822	3. The first tabbable element in the parent scope
823	4. `document.body` as the final fallback
824	
825	## 9. Platform Notes
826	
827	> **Dioxus focus operations:** Focus operations (`focus_first`, `focus_last`,
828	> `restore_focus_safely`) use `PlatformEffects` trait methods (see `01-architecture.md`
829	> section 2.2.7). For Dioxus Desktop/Mobile, the adapter provides a platform implementation
830	> that routes these through native focus APIs for cross-platform compatibility.
831	>
832	> **Cleanup timing:** Leptos uses `on_cleanup` and Dioxus uses `use_drop` for teardown.
833	> In HMR/hot-reload scenarios, timing may differ — ensure the `FocusRestorationStack`
834	> is cleared on both cleanup and re-mount.
835	
836	## 10. Library Parity
837	
838	> Compared against: React Aria (`FocusScope`).
839	
840	### 10.1 Props
841	
842	| Feature       | ars-ui                | React Aria     | Notes                                    |
843	| ------------- | --------------------- | -------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
844	| Auto-focus    | `auto_focus`          | `autoFocus`    | Both libraries                           |
845	| Contain/trap  | `trapped` / `contain` | `contain`      | Both libraries; ars-ui offers both names |
846	| Restore focus | `restore_focus`       | `restoreFocus` | Both libraries                           |
847	
848	**Gaps:** None.
849	
850	### 10.2 Anatomy
851	
852	| Part      | ars-ui      | React Aria    | Notes                                  |
853	| --------- | ----------- | ------------- | -------------------------------------- |
854	| Container | `Container` | (wrapper div) | Both libraries use a container element |
855	
856	**Gaps:** None.
857	
858	### 10.3 Features
859	
860	| Feature                  | ars-ui                              | React Aria              |
861	| ------------------------ | ----------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
862	| Focus trapping           | Yes                                 | Yes                     |
863	| Focus restoration        | Yes                                 | Yes                     |
864	| Auto-focus first element | Yes                                 | Yes                     |
865	| FocusManager context     | Yes (`focus_next`/`focus_previous`) | Yes (`useFocusManager`) |
866	| Nested scope stack       | Yes (`FocusRestorationStack`)       | Yes (internal)          |
867	| Focus navigation options | Yes (`FocusNavigationOptions`)      | Yes (`wrap`, etc.)      |
868	
869	**Gaps:** None.
870	
871	### 10.4 Summary
872	
873	- **Overall:** Full parity.
874	- **Divergences:** ars-ui exposes both `trapped` and `contain` prop aliases. ars-ui explicitly defines `FocusRestorationStack` for nested scopes; React Aria handles this internally.
875	- **Recommended additions:** None.
```

> AGENT

All gates still green after removal.

## Summary of the removal

**Code changes (`crates/ars-components/src/utility/focus_scope/mod.rs`):**
- Removed `Api::focus_next` and `Api::focus_previous` methods (the "always returns `false`" stubs)
- Removed `pub type AcceptFn = Rc<dyn Fn(&str) -> bool>`
- Removed `pub struct FocusNavigationOptions` + its `Debug` + `Default` impls
- Dropped the unused `alloc::rc::Rc` import
- Removed two unit tests that exercised the stubs

**Spec changes (`spec/components/utility/focus-scope.md`):**
- §1.7 Api block: dropped the `focus_next` / `focus_previous` methods, the `FocusNavigationOptions` struct, the `AcceptFn` alias, and their `Debug` / `Default` impls
- §1.7 (new prose): a paragraph immediately after the API block explaining why programmatic sequential navigation lives in the adapter layer (synchronous DOM lookups, live element handles), linking back to the issue's "Element/ref handling note"
- §1.7 Focus Manager Context: clarified that `focus_first` / `focus_last` are thin wrappers over the core's `Api::focus_first` / `Api::focus_last` (which emit `Effect::FocusFirst` / `Effect::FocusLast`), whereas `focus_next` / `focus_previous` plus any `FocusNavigationOptions` config struct are entirely adapter-owned
- §9 Platform Notes: stopped mentioning the removed `restore_focus_safely` symbol and re-anchored the platform-notes intro on the named effects + the §1.6.1 safety chain
- §10.3 parity table: changed the "FocusManager context" and "Focus navigation options" rows to "Adapter-only" with a short explanation
- §10.4 Divergences: noted that programmatic sequential navigation is an intentional adapter-layer divergence

**Verification (re-run):**

```
cargo nextest run -p ars-components focus_scope  →  50/50 passed
cargo clippy -p ars-components --all-targets -- -D warnings  →  no warnings
cargo insta test -p ars-components --all-features --unreferenced=reject  →  no orphans
cargo xtask spec validate  →  340 files validated
cargo xtask ci spec-compile-snippets  →  2173 blocks parsed, no findings
cargo mutants -p ars-components -f .../focus_scope/mod.rs  →  22 caught, 11 unviable, 0 missed
```

Net: agnostic-core `Api` surface shrinks by two methods + one struct + one type alias; spec gains a precise explanation of where the deleted pieces belong instead. Ready for your final review before `cargo xci --profile fast` and commit/push.

595	fn handle_effects<M: Machine + 'static>(
596	    send_result: ars_core::SendResult<M>,
597	    ctx: &M::Context,
598	    props: &M::Props,
599	    send_ref: SendCallbackRef<M>,
600	    effect_cleanups: EffectCleanupStore<M>,
601	) where
602	    M::State: Clone + PartialEq + Send + Sync + 'static,
603	    M::Context: Clone + Send + Sync + 'static,
604	    M::Props: Clone + PartialEq + Send + Sync + 'static,
605	    M::Event: Send + Sync + 'static,
606	{
607	    if send_result.state_changed {
608	        effect_cleanups.update_value(|cleanups| {
609	            for (_, cleanup) in cleanups.drain() {
610	                cleanup();
611	            }
612	        });
613	    } else if !send_result.cancel_effects.is_empty() || !send_result.pending_effects.is_empty() {
614	        effect_cleanups.update_value(|cleanups| {
615	            for name in send_result.cancel_effects.iter() {
616	                if let Some(cleanup) = cleanups.remove(name) {
617	                    cleanup();
618	                }
619	            }
620	
621	            for effect in &send_result.pending_effects {
622	                if let Some(cleanup) = cleanups.remove(&effect.name) {
623	                    cleanup();
624	                }
625	            }
626	        });
627	    }
628	
629	    if send_result.pending_effects.is_empty() {
630	        return;
631	    }
632	
633	    if let Some(send_callback) = send_ref.with_value(|slot| slot.as_ref().copied()) {
634	        let send_handle = callback_to_strong_send(send_callback);
635	
636	        for effect in send_result.pending_effects {
637	            let name = effect.name;
638	
639	            let cleanup = effect.run(ctx, props, Arc::clone(&send_handle));
640	
641	            effect_cleanups.update_value(|cleanups| {
642	                cleanups.insert(name, cleanup);
643	            });
644	        }
645	    }
646	}
647	
648	#[cfg(test)]
649	mod test_support;
```

> AGENT

Codex found a real P1 bug. The adapter's `handle_effects` drains all active cleanups when `state_changed` is true (`ars-leptos/src/use_machine/mod.rs:607-612`). My `TrapFocus`/`ReleaseTrap` transitions only change state without re-emitting `Effect::FocusTrapListener`, so the trap listener is torn down and never reinstalled — Tab trapping silently breaks until a full deactivate/activate cycle.

Writing the failing test first per TDD.

  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn release_trap_toggles_trapped_flag_within_active() {\n        let mut service = service(test_props());\n\n        drop(service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: true,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        }));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::ReleaseTrap);\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert_eq!(service.state(), &State::Active { trapped: false });\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn trap_focus_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change() {\n        // Regression guard for Codex review #663: adapters drain ALL active\n        // effect cleanups whenever `state_changed` is true (see\n        // `ars-leptos::use_machine::handle_effects` line 607). If the\n        // `Active{trapped:false} → Active{trapped:true}` transition does\n        // NOT re-emit `Effect::FocusTrapListener`, the adapter tears down\n        // the keydown trap and never reinstalls it, silently breaking Tab\n        // trapping until a full deactivate/activate cycle.\n        let mut service = service(test_props());\n        drop(service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: false,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        }));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::TrapFocus);\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert!(\n            effect_names(&result.pending_effects).contains(&Effect::FocusTrapListener),\n            \"TrapFocus must re-emit Effect::FocusTrapListener so the adapter reinstalls \\\n             the trap listener after the state-change-driven cleanup drain\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn release_trap_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change() {\n        // Same regression class as\n        // `trap_focus_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change`,\n        // but for the `Active{trapped:true} → Active{trapped:false}`\n        // direction. The trap listener semantically stays valid across\n        // both Active variants — its `trapped` check reads context — but\n        // the adapter still needs to be told to reinstall after the\n        // state-change cleanup drain.\n        let mut service = service(test_props());\n        drop(service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: true,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        }));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::ReleaseTrap);\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert!(\n            effect_names(&result.pending_effects).contains(&Effect::FocusTrapListener),\n            \"ReleaseTrap must re-emit Effect::FocusTrapListener so the adapter reinstalls \\\n             the trap listener after the state-change-driven cleanup drain\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn trap_focus_ignored_when_already_trapped() {"

590	| Effect              | When emitted                                                                                                   | Adapter contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
591	| ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
592	| `FocusTrapListener` | `Inactive → Active` (Activate)                                                                                 | Call `PlatformEffects::attach_focus_trap(container_id, on_escape)`. Wire the returned `CleanupFn` as the effect's cleanup so `cancel_effect(Effect::FocusTrapListener)` tears the handler down on `Deactivate`. The `on_escape` callback SHOULD send `Event::Deactivate { restore_focus: true }`. |
593	| `FocusFirst`        | `Active + FocusFirst` (also on `Activate` when `props.auto_focus`)                                             | Call `PlatformEffects::focus_first_tabbable(container_id)`. Fall back to focusing the container element (which has `tabindex="-1"` while active) when no tabbable descendants exist.                                                                                                              |
594	| `FocusLast`         | `Active + FocusLast`                                                                                           | Call `PlatformEffects::focus_last_tabbable(container_id)`. Fall back to the container when no tabbable descendants exist.                                                                                                                                                                         |
595	| `RestoreFocus`      | `Active → Inactive` (Deactivate with `restore_focus: true`) or explicit `Event::RestoreFocus` while `Inactive` | Read `service.context().saved_focus`. If `Some(id)`, apply the §1.6.1 / §7 fallback chain via `PlatformEffects::can_restore_focus` → `focus_element_by_id` → `nearest_focusable_ancestor_id` → `focus_body`. The core does not clear `saved_focus` here; the next `Activate` overwrites it.       |

Wire the returned `CleanupFn` as the effect's cleanup so `cancel_effect(Effect::FocusTrapListener)` tears the handler down on `Deactivate`. {
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(focus-scope): re-emit FocusTrapListener on TrapFocus/ReleaseTrap (Codex review #663)\n\nAdapters drain ALL active effect cleanups whenever `state_changed` is true\n(see `ars-leptos/src/use_machine/mod.rs:607-612`). The original\n`Active{trapped:false} ↔ Active{trapped:true}` transitions only changed\nstate and emitted no effects, which torn down the Tab keydown trap and\nnever reinstalled it — silently breaking trapping until a full\ndeactivate/activate cycle.\n\nBoth `TrapFocus` and `ReleaseTrap` now re-emit\n`PendingEffect::named(Effect::FocusTrapListener)` so the adapter\nreinstalls the listener after the cleanup drain.\n\nSpec §1.5 / §1.6 / §1.8 updated to document the re-emission trigger and\nits adapter-cleanup-drain rationale.\n\nTests: two new regression guards\n(`trap_focus_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change`,\n`release_trap_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change`)\nthat fail on the pre-fix transition and pass after it. Mutation testing\nre-ran: 22 caught, 11 unviable, 0 missed (same as before).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit the Codex-flagged fix"
}

> AGENT

**Round 1 of Codex review handled:**

- **Finding (P1):** `TrapFocus` / `ReleaseTrap` only changed state without re-emitting `Effect::FocusTrapListener`. Adapters drain all active effect cleanups on `state_changed` (verified at `ars-leptos/src/use_machine/mod.rs:607-612`) → keydown trap was silently torn down on every trap toggle.
- **Fix (`8d19ab7b`):** Both transitions now re-emit `PendingEffect::named(Effect::FocusTrapListener)` so the adapter reinstalls the listener after the cleanup drain. Spec §1.5 / §1.6 / §1.8 updated.
- **Tests:** Two new regression guards (`trap_focus_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change`, `release_trap_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change`) confirmed red on the old code, green on the fix.
- **Verification:** `cargo nextest` 52/52 pass, `cargo clippy -D warnings` clean, `cargo xtask spec validate` + `spec-compile-snippets` green, `cargo mutants` still 22 caught / 11 unviable / 0 missed, `cargo xci --profile fast` all 10 gates pass.
- **Thread resolved**, `@codex review` posted, watcher polling for the next pass.

  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn activate_propagates_contain_prop_into_trapped_when_event_trapped_false() {\n        let mut service = service(test_props().contain(true));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: false,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        });\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert_eq!(\n            service.state(),\n            &State::Active { trapped: true },\n            \"contain=true must promote `trapped` even when event passes false\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn activate_propagates_trapped_prop_into_trapped_when_event_trapped_false() {\n        // Regression guard for Codex review #663 (P2): `Props::trapped`\n        // is documented in spec §1.4 as the prop that prevents Tab from\n        // escaping. The original `Activate` transition ORed only the\n        // event's `trapped` and `props.contain`, silently dropping\n        // `props.trapped` and forcing every caller to mirror it into\n        // `Event::Activate { trapped: ... }`. Adapters that activate\n        // with a constant `false` (or that derive `trapped` solely from\n        // a transient interaction state) would have left a\n        // `Props { trapped: true, .. }` scope untrapped.\n        let mut service = service(test_props().trapped(true));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: false,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        });\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert_eq!(\n            service.state(),\n            &State::Active { trapped: true },\n            \"Props::trapped=true must promote `trapped` even when the event passes false\",\n        );\n    }"

Adapters MAY send this event\n+    /// explicitly when an `Inactive` scope still holds a saved target\n+    /// (e.g., nested-scope cleanup).\n+    RestoreFocus,\n+\n+    /// Request focus on the first tabbable element inside the container.\n+    /// Emits [`Effect::FocusFirst`] when `Active`; ignored otherwise.\n+    FocusFirst,\n+\n+    /// Request focus on the last tabbable element inside the container.\n+    /// Emits [`Effect::FocusLast`] when `Active`; ignored otherwise.\n+    FocusLast,\n+}\n+\n+// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n+// Context\n+// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n+\n+/// Machine context for the `FocusScope` component.\n+///\n+/// `is_active()` and `is_trapped()` are derived from [`State`] in the\n+/// connect API — they are not stored here.\n+#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]\n+pub struct Context {\n+    /// The element that had focus before the scope was activated.\n+    ///\n+    /// Set on [`Event::Activate`] and read by the adapter when dispatching\n+    /// [`Effect::RestoreFocus`]. Adapters set this from a hydration-safe\n+    /// stable ID generator.\n+    pub id: String,\n+\n+    /// Prevent Tab from moving focus outside the container.\n+    pub trapped: bool,\n+\n+    /// Alias for [`trapped`](Self::trapped) — clearer naming in some\n+    /// contexts (e.g., when `trapped` already means \"active\" elsewhere).\n+    pub contain: bool,\n+\n+    /// On activation, automatically move focus to the first tabbable\n+    /// element (or the element with `autofocus` attribute, if any).\n+    pub auto_focus: bool,\n+\n+    /// On deactivation, restore focus to the > AGENT

Both findings are real and related. The trap listener should be installed only when `trapped: true`. My earlier ReleaseTrap fix over-corrected — Codex's first-pass advice was incomplete; the second pass caught the over-correction. Writing two failing tests.

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn trap_focus_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change() {\n        // Regression guard for Codex review #663: adapters drain ALL active\n        // effect cleanups whenever `state_changed` is true (see\n        // `ars-leptos::use_machine::handle_effects` line 607). If the\n        // `Active{trapped:false} → Active{trapped:true}` transition does\n        // NOT re-emit `Effect::FocusTrapListener`, the adapter tears down\n        // the keydown trap and never reinstalls it, silently breaking Tab\n        // trapping until a full deactivate/activate cycle.\n        let mut service = service(test_props());\n        drop(service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: false,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        }));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::TrapFocus);\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert!(\n            effect_names(&result.pending_effects).contains(&Effect::FocusTrapListener),\n            \"TrapFocus must re-emit Effect::FocusTrapListener so the adapter reinstalls \\\n             the trap listener after the state-change-driven cleanup drain\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn release_trap_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change() {\n        // Same regression class as\n        // `trap_focus_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change`,\n        // but for the `Active{trapped:true} → Active{trapped:false}`\n        // direction. The trap listener semantically stays valid across\n        // both Active variants — its `trapped` check reads context — but\n        // the adapter still needs to be told to reinstall after the\n        // state-change cleanup drain.\n        let mut service = service(test_props());\n        drop(service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: true,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        }));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::ReleaseTrap);\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert!(\n            effect_names(&result.pending_effects).contains(&Effect::FocusTrapListener),\n            \"ReleaseTrap must re-emit Effect::FocusTrapListener so the adapter reinstalls \\\n             the trap listener after the state-change-driven cleanup drain\",\n        );\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn trap_focus_re_emits_focus_trap_listener_so_adapter_reinstalls_after_state_change() {\n        // Regression guard for Codex review #663 round 1 (P1): adapters\n        // drain ALL active effect cleanups whenever `state_changed` is\n        // true (see `ars-leptos::use_machine::handle_effects` line 607).\n        // `Active{trapped:false} → Active{trapped:true}` enters a state\n        // that DOES need the listener, so the transition must re-emit\n        // `Effect::FocusTrapListener` to reinstall the trap after the\n        // drain, otherwise Tab interception is silently disabled.\n        let mut service = service(test_props());\n        drop(service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: false,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        }));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::TrapFocus);\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert!(\n            effect_names(&result.pending_effects).contains(&Effect::FocusTrapListener),\n            \"TrapFocus moves into a trapped state — Effect::FocusTrapListener must \\\n             be re-emitted so the adapter reinstalls the listener after the drain\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn release_trap_does_not_emit_focus_trap_listener_so_drain_can_uninstall() {\n        // Regression guard for Codex review #663 round 2 (P1):\n        // `Active{trapped:true} → Active{trapped:false}` leaves the\n        // scope active but no longer trapping. The adapter's\n        // `state_changed`-driven drain runs the listener cleanup, and\n        // the transition MUST NOT re-emit `Effect::FocusTrapListener`\n        // — otherwise the adapter immediately reinstalls the trap and\n        // `ReleaseTrap` becomes a no-op (Tab stays intercepted even\n        // though state says it shouldn't).\n        let mut service = service(test_props());\n        drop(service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: true,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        }));\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::ReleaseTrap);\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert!(\n            !effect_names(&result.pending_effects).contains(&Effect::FocusTrapListener),\n            \"ReleaseTrap moves out of a trapped state — Effect::FocusTrapListener \\\n             must NOT be re-emitted, so the drain can uninstall the listener\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn activate_does_not_emit_focus_trap_listener_when_resolved_trap_is_false() {\n        // Regression guard for Codex review #663 round 2 (P1):\n        // Activating into `Active{trapped:false}` (no trap requested by\n        // event/props) must NOT install the trap listener — the scope\n        // is active for focus-restoration accounting but does not\n        // intercept Tab. Emitting the effect would have the adapter\n        // attach Tab interception unconditionally via\n        // `PlatformEffects::attach_focus_trap`, contradicting the\n        // resolved `trapped: false` state.\n        let mut service = service(test_props()); // trapped=false, contain=false\n\n        let result = service.send(Event::Activate {\n            trapped: false,\n            saved_focus_id: None,\n        });\n\n        assert!(result.state_changed);\n        assert_eq!(service.state(), &State::Active { trapped: false });\n        assert!(\n            !effect_names(&result.pending_effects).contains(&Effect::FocusTrapListener),\n            \"Activate into Active{{trapped:false}} must NOT emit \\\n             Effect::FocusTrapListener — the adapter would otherwise install \\\n             Tab interception despite the resolved untrapped state\",\n        );\n    }"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7b271fe9/spec/components/utility/focus-scope.md",
  "old_string": "| `FocusTrapListener` | `Inactive → Active` (Activate) and every `Active{trapped:_} ↔ Active{trapped:_}` transition (TrapFocus / ReleaseTrap) | Call `PlatformEffects::attach_focus_trap(container_id, on_escape)`. Wire the returned `CleanupFn` as the effect's cleanup so `cancel_effect(Effect::FocusTrapListener)` tears the handler down on `Deactivate`. The `on_escape` callback SHOULD send `Event::Deactivate { restore_focus: true }`. **Re-emitted on TrapFocus / ReleaseTrap because adapters drain all active cleanups when `state_changed` is true, so the listener must be reinstalled to preserve Tab trapping across the Active variants.** |",
  "new_string": "| `FocusTrapListener` | `Inactive → Active{trapped:true}` (Activate when the resolved trap is `true`) and `Active{trapped:false} → Active{trapped:true}` (TrapFocus). NOT emitted on `Inactive → Active{trapped:false}` (active-but-untrapped activation) or on `ReleaseTrap` — the listener is only valid while `trapped: true`. | Call `PlatformEffects::attach_focus_trap(container_id, on_escape)`. Wire the returned `CleanupFn` as the effect's cleanup so `cancel_effect(Effect::FocusTrapListener)` tears the handler down on `Deactivate`. The `on_escape` callback SHOULD send `Event::Deactivate { restore_focus: true }`. The state-change-driven cleanup drain also uninstalls the listener whenever the resolved state moves off `trapped: true` (TrapFocus reinstalls it, ReleaseTrap and Deactivate intentionally leave it uninstalled). |"
}

{
  "command": "git add -A && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(focus-scope): gate FocusTrapListener on resolved trapped state (Codex review #663 round 2)\n\nTwo related P1 findings: both `Activate` (always emitted the effect) and\n`ReleaseTrap` (was changed in round 1 to re-emit on the state-change drain)\nwere installing the Tab keydown trap in states that should NOT have it.\n`PlatformEffects::attach_focus_trap` unconditionally wraps Tab/Shift+Tab,\nso emitting the effect for `Active{trapped:false}` contradicts the\nresolved untrapped state — `ReleaseTrap` became a no-op (Tab stayed\nintercepted) and an active-but-untrapped activation trapped Tab anyway.\n\nFix:\n\n- `Activate`: emit `Effect::FocusTrapListener` only when the resolved\n  `trap` boolean is `true`. Active-but-untrapped activations install\n  no listener.\n- `ReleaseTrap`: do not emit the effect — the `state_changed=true`\n  drain uninstalls the listener, which is the desired outcome.\n- `TrapFocus`: keeps re-emitting (round 1 fix), since it enters a\n  trapped state that needs the listener reinstalled after the drain.\n\nSpec §1.5 / §1.6 / §1.8 updated to document the resolved-trap gating.\n\nTwo new regression guards:\n- `release_trap_does_not_emit_focus_trap_listener_so_drain_can_uninstall`\n- `activate_does_not_emit_focus_trap_listener_when_resolved_trap_is_false`\n\nBoth fail on the pre-fix transitions and pass after. Mutation rerun:\n23 caught / 11 unviable / 0 missed.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit two P1 fixes"
}
